First route review: no mechanism for lighter-than-Gaussian tails; per-level gluing fails

16 October 2026 · Route review; no statement registered · First route review. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

The line's general claim. The thread tests Talagrand's Ultimate Matching Conjecture (conj:ultimate-matching) on its whole curve \(1/\alpha_1+1/\alpha_2=1/2\). Its working route is the Hopf–Lax reduction: the conjecture at every pair, with one constant, follows from conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy, a uniform discrepancy bound \(\frac L2\sqrt{N\log N}\) over the class of functions with \(\int\sum_j\psi^*_{\alpha_j}(|\partial_jG|)\le2\); in primal form (lem:flow-matching-bound), from one flow between the smeared configurations with Orlicz cost of order \(N\).

What the goal needs. A proof at every pair of the curve with a universal \(L\) (the evenly-spread form suffices by lem:two-sample-matching-transfer), or a refutation at one pair: for every \(L\), with probability bounded away from one no good matching.

The six entries

The monograph reading fixed the statement and proved the transfer, the consequences and the common exponent cap. The Hopf–Lax reduction gave the sufficient discrepancy statement (the only entry marked advanced, as a conditional reduction). The Leighton–Shor class test showed the reduced statement unrefuted on the curve and attained in order there. The dyadic flow bound calibrated the primal form on the off-curve pair \((2,2)\); the dyadic flow obstruction proved that this flow cannot reach any pair with an exponent above \(2\); the below-curve exclusion proved that no pair below the curve holds, in both settings, for every constant.

Falsification attempt. The strongest available attempt to refute the conjecture on the curve is the below-curve exclusion's argument evaluated on the curve: the scaled Leighton–Shor excess and the anisotropic boundary layer are both of order \(\sqrt N(\log N)^{1/2+\beta_2}\), so the argument decides nothing there, and sharper constants could only bound the conjecture's constant from below. The queued item max-displacement-curve-barrier records the reviewer's conditional lead: if the Leighton–Shor upper bound holds on rectangles of aspect ratio up to \((\log N)^{1/2}\) with a universal constant (unchecked), no argument using only maximal displacements can do better. No refutation was found.

Assessment of this line. The line has settled what lies around the conjecture and nothing of the conjecture itself. It is now known, with proofs, that the curve cannot be lowered anywhere (below-curve exclusion), that the reduced discrepancy statement is attained in order on the curve (Leighton–Shor class test), that the primal flow route reproduces the known pair \((2,2)\), and that its one explicit flow fails above exponent \(2\) because its per-point values are Gaussian. Four entries in a row left the goal unchanged; the one advance is a sufficient reduction to a statement nobody knows how to bound. Upper bounds on the curve have no mechanism on record: every construction tried is linear in the empirical measure and Gaussian per point, while every pair of the curve has an exponent above \(2\) in some coordinate, which needs lighter than Gaussian tails at scale \(\sqrt{\log N/N}\) there (or a bounded displacement, at an infinite exponent).

Obstacle

The line is stuck on one point: there is no construction of a matching, or of a flow, whose displacement at a typical point has tails lighter than Gaussian at scale \(\sqrt{\log N/N}\), in a coordinate whose exponent exceeds \(2\), while its average displacement stays at the Ajtai–Komlós–Tusnády order. Every mechanism on record (the Ajtai–Komlós–Tusnády transformation, the dyadic flow, Talagrand–Yukich's fixed-point martingale, flows linear in the empirical measure) sums independent level contributions at a point and is Gaussian there; the Leighton–Shor mechanism controls the maximum but says nothing of the joint exponential moments; [T4]'s discrepancy approach loses by bounding a supremum of a sum by a sum of suprema; and, by the reviewer's counterexample below, per-level Hall conditions cannot be glued into one matching. A refutation, conversely, would have to use the joint exponential moments if the conditional barrier above holds.

Outside leads

  • Alweiss, Liu and Sawhney, "Discrepancy minimization via a self-balancing walk" (STOC 2021; recalled): online signing of vectors with each coordinate's running sum kept at \(O(\sqrt{\log(nT)})\), against \(\sqrt T\) for random signs, by biasing each sign against the current sum. It targets conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy through its primal form: build the flow level by level and, within each dyadic square, choose the divergence-free part of the four-child flux profile to anti-correlate with the coarser levels' field. Answers. It would replace the Gaussian per-point sum of independent level contributions, the obstacle, by an adaptively balanced one, the only kind of flow the dyadic flow obstruction leaves open. Test. Not run: a calculation checked a prerequisite of the translation, not its break point. Inside a square \(S\), with given boundary fluxes and divergence, \(\int_S\Phi_k=\int_{\partial S}x_k\,\Phi\cdot n-\int_Sx_k\operatorname{div}\Phi\), so the free part \(\nabla^\perp\psi\), \(\psi\in H^1_0(S)\), has mean zero; whether such corrections can lower the per-point tails, balancing at a point being paid for elsewhere in the square, needs a simulation, the decisive test below.
  • The linearization of random optimal transport in the plane (Ambrosio, Stra and Trevisan, "A PDE approach to a 2-dimensional matching problem", Probab. Theory Related Fields 2019; recalled, not read): the quadratic-cost optimal map is close to the identity plus the gradient of the solution of a Poisson equation with the regularized empirical measure as data. It targets the refutation side of ultimate-matching and the shape of any proof: if the canonical matching is, per point, a linear flow, the conjecture's matchings must be far from quadratic-optimal. Answers. It locates the obstacle: a proof would have to use a nonlinear matching. Test. Not run: the linearization was recalled, not read. A calculation on the linear model only: the electric flow regularized at length \(\delta\asymp s\) has kernel bounded by about \(1/\delta\) and per-point variance \(N\log(1/\delta)/(2\pi)\asymp N\log N\), so lem:block-lower-tail gives Gaussian lower tails at a point up to \(\sqrt{\log N}\) standard deviations, as for the dyadic flow; this concerns the route's own model, not the translation, whose break point is that the linearization is an \(L^2\) statement, not a pointwise one.
  • Talagrand's chaining bounds for empirical processes under Bernstein-type mixed tails (the \(\gamma_1+\gamma_2\) bounds of [T4]; recalled, not read in this cycle): \(\mathbb E\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}|\sum_i(f(X_i)-\mathbb Ef)|\lesssim\sqrt N\gamma_2(\mathcal F,d_2)+\gamma_1(\mathcal F,d_\infty)\). It targets conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy directly. Answers. It would bound the supremum over the whole class at once, avoiding the sum-of-suprema loss named in the obstacle, if the class's two functionals can be computed. Test. Not run: the functionals of the conjugate class were not computed, and the monograph reading recorded that the analogous question for Shor's class is [T4]'s open Research Problem 17.2.2, so no cheap test exists.

Absurd bridges

  • The Holtsmark distribution of the field of random point charges (statistical physics and astronomy; Chandrasekhar, "Stochastic problems in physics and astronomy", Rev. Mod. Phys. 1943; recalled). Translation: the excess of \(X\) over the uniform background is a gas of independent unit charges, a linear flow at a point is the field of that gas through a kernel decaying like \(1/r\), and the matching chooses which charges neutralize which, as a plasma screens. The theorem there (as recalled): the field of independent charges with kernel \(r^{-p}\) in dimension \(d\) is stable of index \(d/p\), Gaussian with a logarithmic variance at \(d/p=2\); screening makes it short-range. Answers. It explains the obstacle as the borderline case \(d/p=2\) and names a cure, screening. Test. Not run for the translation: the calculation done, the unscreened variance \(N\int_\delta^1r^{-2}\,2\pi r\,dr=2\pi N\log(1/\delta)\asymp N\log N\) at \(\delta\asymp s\), confirms the borderline prediction but not the screening translation, whose break point is that Gauss's law fixes the flux \(\sqrt N(\log N)^{3/4}\) through the Leighton–Shor curves whatever the screening.
  • The divisible sandpile and its odometer (Levine and Peres, "Strong spherical asymptotics for rotor-router aggregation and the divisible sandpile", Potential Anal. 2009; recalled). Translation: put the excess of \(X\) over the background on a grid as sand and topple; the odometer \(u\) solves an obstacle problem, and \(\nabla u\) is a flow nonlinear in the data, unlike the dyadic and electric flows. Answers. It is a natural adaptive flow, the kind the obstacle requires. Test. Not run: in dimension one every flow equals the cumulative excess, so the smallest informative test is a two-dimensional simulation of the odometer's per-point tails.
  • The power of two choices in load balancing (Azar, Broder, Karlin and Upfal, 1994; recalled): placing each ball in the less loaded of two random bins lowers the maximum load from \(\log n/\log\log n\) to \(\log\log n\). Translation: route each unit of excess along the less loaded of two candidate dyadic paths. Answers. Two choices turn Poisson-type tails into doubly exponential ones, the tail lightening the obstacle asks for. Test. Not run: the routing must keep the flow's divergence, and the smallest test is a simulation of per-point loads.

No item passed its test, so none is picked. Since the review describes the upper bound as without a mechanism on record, the Not run items bearing on it get development cycles: the self-balancing lead is the decisive test below, and the sandpile odometer and the two-choice routing join the queue as checks; the chaining lead's question is [T4]'s open Research Problem 17.2.2 in another form and is not queued separately.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For every pair \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in[2,\infty]\) with \(1/\alpha_1+1/\alpha_2=1/2\) and one universal \(L\): with probability tending to one, for \(X\) i.i.d. uniform and \(Y\) evenly spread, a matching good in both coordinates; or, to refute, one pair at which for every \(L\) the probability of such a matching stays bounded away from one. Downstream. Through lem:two-sample-matching-transfer the evenly-spread target gives the two-sample goal with constant \(2L\): the goal itself. Through cor:exponential-matching-discrepancy-reduction, conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy gives the evenly-spread target at every pair with one constant: the goal. On the refutation side thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion gives exclusion only strictly below the curve, which is weaker than a refutation.

Extra requirements. The route asks for a uniform bound over a whole class of test functions, with cell smearing, at a fixed constant. The reviewer's stage-one observation that the two coordinates' constraints merge into one transport cost at a constant loss, with Kantorovich duality, suggests that a class supremum is unavoidable for the exact problem; the demand is then necessary up to the open converse of the Hopf–Lax bound (sub-idea entry-2026-10-16-hopf-lax-reduction:s1). In primal form the route asks for one flow serving every exponential level with one Orlicz cost; the stage-one alternative (A) tried to weaken this to one deficiency bound per level, and the counterexample below shows that weakening loses an unbounded factor.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the route, the medium reviewer received the goal, the known results and the registered statements only (stage-one record). Its key observation: in layer-cake form the conjecture asks, for each level \(k\le\log N\), that at most about \(Ne^{-k}\) pairs leave the box \(sk^{1/\alpha_1}\times sk^{1/\alpha_2}\), whose area \(s^2\sqrt k\) is the same for every pair of the curve; only the aspect ratio changes. It proposed, ranked: (A) a Hall deficiency bound per level glued by a deterministic lemma for nested bipartite graphs, noting itself that the gluing is equivalent to one transport bound with a layered cost and so circular as a reduction; (B) Shor's coordinate-separated strips for \((2,\infty)\), which it showed must exchange mass at every horizontal scale; (C) randomized transport plans with per-level martingales, close to the current flow route; (D) Leighton–Shor subgraphs whose aspect ratio varies with the scale, a maximal-displacement refutation argument and so a test of the conditional barrier above. The route's registered open conjectures and their evidence, beside the proved results: conj:ultimate-matching (open; the curve cannot be lowered, thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion; the off-curve pair \((2,2)\) holds, thm:dyadic-flow-square-exponential); conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy (open; attained in order on the curve, lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound; no upper-bound mechanism; its one explicit primal flow fails above exponent \(2\), thm:dyadic-flow-obstruction). Comparison after the second stage: (A) collapses into the current formulation (counterexample below); (C) is the current route; (B) addresses only the endpoint \((2,\infty)\); (D) remains a refutation test of the barrier, queued.

Per-level gluing fails (the reviewer's counterexample, checked here). Take \(X=\{1,\dots,n\}\) and \(Y=X+h\) on a line, levels \(G_k=\{(x,y):|x-y|\le e^{k-1}\}\), \(k=1,\dots,K\) with \(e^{K-1}\ge n+h\), weights \(w_k=e^k\). For \(e^{k-1}<h\) the deficiency of \(G_k\) is \(m_k=h-\lfloor e^{k-1}\rfloor\le h\) (all of \(X\) has \(n-h+\lfloor e^{k-1}\rfloor\) neighbours, and the shift by \(\lfloor e^{k-1}\rfloor\) matches that many), and \(m_k=0\) once \(e^{k-1}\ge h\); so \(\sum_kw_km_k\le h\sum_{e^{k-1}<h}e^k\le e^2h^2/(e-1)\). An edge of length \(d\ge2\) costs \(\sum_{k:e^{k-1}<d}e^k\ge d\) (the largest such \(k\) has \(e^k\ge d\)), and every perfect matching has \(\sum(y-x)=nh\), so its layered cost is at least \(nh-n\) (edges of length at most \(1\) contribute at most \(n\) to the sum). With \(h=\lfloor\sqrt n\rfloor\) the per-level sum is \(O(n)\) and every matching costs at least \(n(\sqrt n-2)\): no constant-factor gluing exists, even on geometric, deterministic configurations. The reason, as the reviewer put it: a Hall deficiency counts how many pairs leave \(G_k\), not how far they must go; the true lower bound integrates the deficiencies of all level sets of one potential, which by the coarea formula is a Lipschitz-type class supremum, the route's own formulation. The next entry registers this after a full check, with the coarea remark as its own question.

Rank normalization. No dimension counts arise. The analogous question, whether losses and gains are measured in the same units, is what the counterexample settles for (A): per-level counts of pairs leaving a box are the wrong unit, since the cost of a pair grows with its length beyond the box.

Decision. Retain the class formulation (conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy and its primal flow form): the independent alternative's distinct candidate (A) reduces to it, and (B) is endpoint-only. The next entry is lemma-first: register "per-level gluing fails" after checking it, and then run the decisive test below on the one mechanism the obstacle leaves open, adaptive flows. The flow route stays as queue item entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3, whose latest follow-up names the same self-balancing attempt; (D) and the reviewer's "local completion" variant of (A) join the queue as checks.

Decisive test. Trivial cases. The quantities compared are the per-point flow values of two flows with the same divergence. A configuration with no excess at any level gives the zero flow for both; reflecting or rotating the square by a symmetry of the dyadic grid permutes both flows alike; and whatever circulation is chosen inside a parent square, the average of its four children's means equals the parent's mean, so the finest-square means form a tree martingale in both flows. Power. thm:dyadic-flow-obstruction shows the fixed flow is Gaussian per point with variance of order \(N\log N\); lem:block-lower-tail and the dyadic coefficient weights give its tail at \(t\) standard deviations as at least \(e^{-540t^2}\) for \(t\le\sqrt{\log N}\). No upper bound on any adaptive flow is recorded. A simulation at \(N\) up to about \(2^{20}\) points has about \(10\) dyadic levels and resolves per-point tails to about \(4\) standard deviations over \(10^6\) sample points, where \(e^{-t^2/2}\) and \(e^{-t^4/c}\)-type tails differ by orders of magnitude; the outcomes differ there if the greedy correction changes the tail shape and coincide if the correction only rescales the variance. The growth of the \((4,4)\) cost is a weak criterion at these sizes (\(\log N\le14\)), so the tail shape at fixed \(N\) is the primary outcome. Attempt. Tried from the cited statements: the zero-mean constraint (Outside leads, first item) shows that the correction cannot change the average of \(\Phi\) over any square, so its effect on a point is through within-square variation only; whether that variation, chosen greedily against the coarser field, lowers the fourth moment of \(\Phi(x)\) relative to its variance could not be decided from the statements, since it depends on the joint law of the coarse field and the four child excesses, and the attempt stopped there. Test disposition. New operation. Operation. Build, for random uniform samples, the dyadic flow and a greedy adaptive flow that at each level adds to each square's flux profile a divergence-free correction \(\nabla^\perp\psi\), \(\psi\in H^1_0(S)\), from a small fixed basis that includes the circulation among the four children (\(\psi\) nonzero on the internal child boundaries, which moves flux between children while keeping the flux across \(\partial S\)), chosen to minimize the coordinatewise cost \(\sum_j\psi_4(|\Phi_j|/(\lambda's))\) of the running field over the square's sample points. Compare, at fixed \(N\), the per-point tail shape (the slope of the log tail against \(t^2\) and \(t^4\) at \(3\)–\(4\) standard deviations, and the kurtosis, \(3\) for a Gaussian and below \(3\) for lighter tails), and secondarily the growth of the flow-bound cost at \((4,4)\). Prediction. If the adaptive flow's per-point tails are lighter than Gaussian with its average unchanged, the next entries try to prove a bound for it, a candidate mechanism for conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy; if its tails stay Gaussian, this greedy rule does not overcome the obstacle, and, with the reviewer's degree-of-freedom remark below, the adaptive mechanism must act on fluxes at scales coarser than one parent square. Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "nested bipartite graphs simultaneous matching deficiency levels laminar threshold cost" (for the gluing step) and, in the previous entries, "self-balancing walk adaptive discrepancy bounded tails" returned no related claim; the review-tests listing of this notebook is empty, this being its first review.

New mathematics. None registered here. The reviewer's counterexample to per-level gluing is recorded above with its check and is registered by the next entry. The leads' calculations (the zero-mean constraint, the Gaussian single-point tail of regularized linear flows, the unscreened variance) are recorded with their items.

Goal progress. Unchanged. Bearing. Nothing at the goal's parameters: the review proves nothing on the curve. It was spent to decide, from the six entries and an independent architecture, whether the route has a mechanism to test; it found that the main alternative collapses into the current formulation and names the adaptive-flow test.

Verification gate. One fresh-context medium reviewer, in the staged architecture brief: stage one saw only the goal, the sources' statements and the registered results (stage-one record); stage two saw the route and the draft (stage-two record, review.txt equivalent) and failed it: the decisive test then posed, a gluing lemma, is false by its counterexample, the three items marked Passed had not tested their translations, the barrier was stated unconditionally, and the alternative was misrepresented. A follow-up with the same reviewer (follow-up record) found every change resolved and the restated counterexample correct, and required a true invariance in the Trivial cases, the circulation in the Operation, the tail shape as primary outcome and a narrower negative prediction, all made. Attempt checked. The reviewer answered the first Attempt's stopping step, the gluing lemma, negatively in a few lines (the counterexample above), so that test was replaced. On the present Attempt it found the stopping point honest and continued it without deciding it: a circulation inside a parent square is one scalar per coordinate, while the four child increments have three degrees of freedom, so greedy circulation leaves about two thirds of the conditional variance at each level, and a martingale form of lem:block-lower-tail would then keep the tails Gaussian; the missing step is a lower-deviation bound for martingales with adaptive increments. Process audit. The reviewer proposed the title, and corrected its own earlier proposal, "sub-Gaussian", which the dyadic flow already is; the title says lighter than Gaussian. It found: a decision resting on an unattempted step that the one-dimensional shift answers; three Passed labels whose own break points said the translation was not reached; a conditional barrier stated as fact; the stage-one report misrepresented; "no mechanism" declared while Not run items bearing on it were neither developed nor queued.

Process assessment. Two mistakes: Passed was given to calculations that checked the route's own model or a prerequisite rather than the translation, and the first decisive test was posed without trying its smallest instance. Prevention. The framework already says a test of a recalled statement or of the named theorem alone is Not run, and that a test answerable in a few lines is a lemma; the staged reviewer caught both. No mechanical check can tell whether a calculation reaches a translation's break point, so none is added; the review-brief requirement stands as the check.

Suggestion checked. Of the first suggestion below, the count of free parameters was checked: inside a parent square the four children exchange flux across four internal half-edges, and each child's net internal flux is fixed by its excess and its share of the flux across \(\partial S\); these four balance conditions have rank three (their sum is the parent's balance), so the exchange has exactly one free parameter, the circulation, and by the integration-by-parts identity of the first Outside lead the children's area-weighted means of each coordinate sum to the parent's whatever its value. Not checked: the claim that greedy circulation leaves about two thirds of the conditional variance, and the martingale lower-deviation bound.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • The decisive test may become a lemma: circulation controls one of three degrees of freedom of the four child increments at each level, so a martingale lower-deviation bound (Freedman-type) with conditional variance bounded below would keep the greedy flow Gaussian, and the adaptive mechanism would have to act at scales coarser than one parent square. Record. lem:block-lower-tail and thm:dyadic-flow-obstruction bear on it (independent sums only); the search "self-balancing walk adaptive discrepancy bounded tails" found no martingale version.
  • A "local completion" form of per-level Hall conditions, in which vertices unmatched at level \(k\) must be matchable within \(G_{k+1}\), escapes the counterexample and may hold for random samples; smallest test, random one-dimensional samples, where the monotone optimum is known exactly. Record. The search "nested bipartite graphs simultaneous matching deficiency levels laminar threshold cost" returned nothing related.

Per-level gluing fails; greedy circulation lowers kurtosis without a growing gap

16 October 2026 · Working proof and finite check; test not decided · Adaptive circulation test. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Chosen. This entry develops the queued check entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3, not the head s2, because the first route review made its named attempt the route's decisive test. Its developments, read before planning: the Hopf–Lax reduction ("The reduction is proved, including the endpoints and the comparison with weights at least linear"), the Leighton–Shor class test ("The reduced statement is not refuted by its first test"), the dyadic flow bound ("The item now has a primal criterion and a calibration") and the dyadic flow obstruction ("The item reaches its spell boundary with this entry and rotates"). It bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy, and through it on ultimate-matching: an adaptive flow with lighter than Gaussian per-point tails is the one mechanism the review left open.

Owed attempt. The latest follow-up named "the self-balancing flow of the reviewer's suggestion": at each dyadic level, choose within each square the four-child flux profile to anti-correlate with the coarser levels' field, and compare its per-point tails with the dyadic flow's, first by a simulation. Made in one form: the simulation below, with the greedy circulation as the adaptive choice, the circulation being the one free scalar of the flux exchange between the four children, and the within-square stream function fixed to the pyramid (other stream functions vanishing on the square's boundary were not tried); the review also asked to try a proof first, made and recorded under Architecture test.

Question. Does a greedy choice of the one free parameter of each square's four-child flux exchange, the circulation, make the per-point values of the flow lighter than Gaussian, with the gap growing as the number of levels grows?

General statement. lem:per-level-gluing-fails, the lemma-first part the review asked for, is an all-parameter counterexample; the simulation's finite check ex:greedy-circulation-tails tests one construction at five sizes.

Per-level gluing fails

Statement. For every \(n\ge4\) there are \(n\) points \(X\) and \(n\) points \(Y\) on the integer line and nested bipartite graphs \(G_k=\{(x,y):|x-y|\le e^{k-1}\}\) between them, \(k_0\le k\le K\) for any integer \(k_0\le1\) (or all integers \(k\le K\)), with \(G_K\) complete, such that, with weights \(w_k=e^k\) and \(m_k\) the deficiency of \(G_k\) (the number of vertices of \(X\) left unmatched by a maximum matching of \(G_k\)), \[\sum_kw_km_k\le\frac{e^2}{e-1}h^2+\frac{e}{e-1}h\quad\text{while}\quad\min_M\sum_kw_k|M\setminus G_k|\ge n(h-1)\] over perfect matchings \(M\), with \(h=\lfloor\sqrt n\rfloor\). So no constant \(C\) satisfies \(\min_M\sum_kw_k|M\setminus G_k|\le C\sum_kw_km_k\) for all nested families, even for point configurations on a line with the box thresholds of the conjecture's layer-cake form.

Proof. Take \(X=\{1,\dots,n\}\), \(Y=X+h\), \(K\) the least integer with \(e^{K-1}\ge n+h\). Deficiency: for \(r=e^{k-1}<h\), every \(y\in Y\) within distance \(r\) of \(X\) satisfies \(y\le n+\lfloor r\rfloor\), so \(X\) has \(n-h+\lfloor r\rfloor\) neighbours and \(m_k\ge h-\lfloor r\rfloor\); the shift \(x\mapsto x+\lfloor r\rfloor\) matches every \(x\ge h+1-\lfloor r\rfloor\) within distance \(\lfloor r\rfloor\le r\), so \(m_k=h-\lfloor r\rfloor\le h\). Once \(e^{k-1}\ge h\), \(\lfloor e^{k-1}\rfloor\ge h\) because \(h\) is an integer, and the shift by \(h\) is a perfect matching of \(G_k\), so \(m_k=0\). Levels with \(e^{k-1}<1\) (that is \(k\le0\)) keep only the edges of length \(0\), which match the \(n-h\) points of \(X\cap Y\), so \(m_k=h\) there. The largest \(k^*\) with \(e^{k^*-1}<h\) has \(e^{k^*}<eh\), so \(\sum_{1\le k}w_km_k\le h\sum_{1\le k\le k^*}e^k<h\,e^{k^*}\frac{e}{e-1}<\frac{e^2}{e-1}h^2\), and the levels \(k\le0\) add at most \(h\sum_{k\le0}e^k=\frac{e}{e-1}h\). Cost: an edge \((x,y)\) lies outside \(G_k\) exactly when \(e^{k-1}<|x-y|\), so it costs \(c(d)=\sum_{k:e^{k-1}<d}e^k\ge0\) with \(d=|x-y|\); for \(d\ge2\) the qualifying \(k\) include those with \(1\le k<1+\ln d\), the largest being \(k^\circ=\lceil\ln d\rceil\ge1\), so \(c(d)\ge e^{k^\circ}\ge d\) (no truncation at \(K\), since \(d\le n+h-1<e^{K-1}\)). Every perfect matching has \(\sum(y-x)=\sum Y-\sum X=nh\), hence \(\sum|y-x|\ge nh\), and its edges of length at most \(1\) contribute at most \(n\) to that sum, so its layered cost, at least \(\sum_{d\ge2}c(d)\ge\sum_{d\ge2}d\), is at least \(nh-n\). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. \(h=1\): the levels \(k\ge1\) have \(m_k=0\) (\(G_1\) contains the shift by one) and the bound \(n(h-1)=0\) is trivial; the statement needs \(h\ge2\), that is \(n\ge4\). With one non-complete level \(G\) and the complete one, the two quantities are equal (a maximum matching of \(G\) extends to a perfect matching with exactly \(m\) edges outside \(G\)), so the failure needs several levels; here \(K\) is about \(\ln n\).

Nonvacuity. \(n=10^6\), \(h=1000\): per-level sum at most \(4.31\times10^6\), every perfect matching costs at least \(9.99\times10^8\).

Structural reason. A Hall deficiency at a level counts how many pairs must leave that level's box, but not how far beyond it they must travel; a uniform shift by \(h\) forces every pair to travel \(h\) while each level beyond radius \(h\) sees no deficiency at all. The reason applies to every architecture that certifies a matching by per-level counts alone, whatever the box shapes: in the plane, to per-level anisotropic Leighton–Shor deficiency bounds without completion lengths, and to any refutation strategy that sums per-level deficiencies; it does not apply to bounds that integrate over the level sets of one potential (the class formulation) or that bound completion lengths.

What it shows. The stage-one architecture of the first route review asked, per level, only how many pairs must leave the level's box; the lemma shows that this count does not control how far those pairs must go. In transport terms, every threshold \(t\in[h,n]\) of the potential \(u(x)=x\) carries a discrepancy \(h\) between \(X\) and \(Y\), and integrating over thresholds gives the cost \(nh\); a correct per-level lower bound must integrate over the level sets of one potential in this way, which by the coarea formula is a class supremum like the route's own formulation (the review's remark, not proved here). The lemma refutes the per-level form with these nested boxes only; a per-level condition that also bounds completion lengths is queued as local-completion-hall.

Greedy circulation: simulation

Setting. Script research/results/ultimate_matching_adaptive_flow_20261016/adaptive_flow_test.py, its expectations committed before each run (the docstring records both rounds); output adaptive_flow_results.json (second run) and first_run_results.json beside it, and the second round's smoke output under smoke/. \(N=16\cdot4^J\) points i.i.d. uniform (multinomial cell counts), \(J=6,\dots,10\) dyadic levels, eight seeds per size, field evaluated at four points per finest cell. The dyadic flow is the notebook's dyadic square flow summed over levels. The adaptive flow adds on every square, level by level from the coarsest, \(c\cdot\nabla^\perp\mathrm{pyr}\), the curl of the pyramid \(\min(t_1,1-t_1,t_2,1-t_2)\) (zero normal flux on the square's boundary, flux exchanged between the four children), with \(c\) minimizing \(\sum_{x}\sum_k(A_k+D_k+cs_k)^4\) over the square's evaluation points, a quartic proxy for the \((4,4)\) cost; evaluation points on a square's diagonals (a fraction \(2/m\) of its \(m^2\) points) get \(0\) in both coordinates, a discretization choice (at the finest level, half of the points). Shape statistics are taken on the per-point values pooled over the eight seeds, raw and divided by the dyadic flow's analytic per-position standard deviation \(\sigma_k(x)=(N\sum_jw_k(t_j(x)))^{1/2}\).

Two runs. The first run had a discretization defect found by the reviewer: evaluation points on a square's diagonals were assigned by argmin to the left and right triangles, so the sampled circulation acted mostly on the second coordinate; the second run gives those points \(0\) in both coordinates. The first run's raw dyadic kurtosis (\(3.08\)–\(3.30\)) had left its band \([2.85,3.15]\) at every size; the reviewer's explanation is that \(\sigma_k(x)\) varies with position, so the pooled law is a scale mixture over positions. The first run's smoke output was not kept; the second's is, and the second round's expectations were written before that smoke run (the script was committed after it, unchanged). The numbers below are the second run's.

Controls. (1) Per-level variance against \(N\) times the mean coefficient weight, levels \(j\ge4\): mean ratios \(0.99\)–\(1.02\) at every level, but \(5\) of \(320\) single-realization values, all at level \(4\) (\(256\) squares), fell outside the pre-registered band \([0.85,1.15]\): the control failed as stated, which is attributed to level-\(4\) sampling error. This control and (2) are blind to signs, so a relative sign error between the coordinates could not have been detected by them; the reviewer checked the signs against the construction directly. (2) Flux across the midline against the raw bottom-half excess: worst error \(0.41\) of the bound \(1.5\cdot2^{-J/2}\sqrt N\). (3) The greedy flow's global average equals the dyadic flow's to \(3\times10^{-16}\); this holds for any pattern with zero sum per square and cannot detect errors within a square. (4) The reference, second round's expectations: the raw dyadic kurtosis was predicted within \(0.12\) of \(3(1+0.8/J)\); observed \(3.08\)–\(3.30\) against \(3.24\)–\(3.40\), off by up to \(0.30\), so the mixture formula failed as stated. This prediction was not blind: the dyadic flow does not depend on the circulation and the seeds were the same, so the second run's raw dyadic values equal the first run's, which were known when it was written. The likelier cause of the failure, per the reviewer (a hand computation, unverified beyond lag one), is that the formula treats the weights of different levels as independent, while \(w(t)\) and \(w(2t\bmod1)\) have correlation about \(-0.22\); with that lag alone the prediction becomes about \(3(1+0.45/J)\), \(3.22\) at \(J=6\) and \(3.13\) at \(J=10\), close to the observed values. Divided by \(\sigma_k(x)\), the dyadic flow's kurtosis is \(2.88\)–\(3.19\), inside \([2.85,3.15]\) except \(3.19\) at \(J=10\), and its slopes \(-0.526\) to \(-0.760\) leave \([-0.6,-0.4]\) in three of ten cases: the slope estimates rest on tail fractions of order \(10^{-4}\) from strongly correlated points and are noisy.

Results. Divided by the dyadic \(\sigma_k(x)\), the greedy flow's kurtosis is \(2.71\)–\(2.89\) against the dyadic flow's \(2.88\)–\(3.19\), lower at every size and in both coordinates; since the greedy flow is divided by the dyadic flow's \(\sigma_k(x)\), not its own, some position mixture remains in it, which can only raise kurtosis, so its values below \(3\) indicate a genuinely platykurtic per-position law; its slopes are \(-0.51\) to \(-0.70\). In the first coordinate, the greedy kurtosis at \(J=6,\dots,10\) is \(2.737, 2.844, 2.865, 2.707, 2.715\), with no trend. The fraction beyond \(3\) standardized units is lower for the greedy flow at every size (for instance \(1.53\times10^{-3}\) against \(2.44\times10^{-3}\) at \(J=10\) in the first coordinate). The greedy flow still moves variance between the coordinates after the tie fix (per-size mean ratios to the dyadic flow \(0.70\)–\(0.76\) in the first coordinate, \(1.16\)–\(1.24\) in the second; single runs \(0.60\)–\(0.80\) and \(1.08\)–\(1.38\)), so the tie defect does not explain it; an untested explanation is that the first coordinate's variance is twice the second's (weights \(2\min(t,1-t)^2\) against \(\min(t,1-t)^2\)), so the summed quartic cost favours lowering the first.

Nonvacuity. Every run satisfies the hypotheses of the test (uniform samples, \(16\) expected points per finest cell, \(J\) levels); a growing gap would have shown as the greedy kurtosis or slope moving away from the reference as \(J\) grows.

Architecture test. Not decided. The decisive test of the first route review predicted either lighter than Gaussian tails for the adaptive flow, with a gap growing in \(J\) (a candidate mechanism), or Gaussian tails (this greedy rule does not overcome the obstacle). The first outcome did not occur: the greedy flow's standardized shape is lighter than the dyadic flow's by a roughly constant amount, with no growth from \(J=6\) to \(J=10\). The second is not established either: the greedy kurtosis sits below the reference at every size, the reference's own bands were partly missed, and the reachable range, \(\log N\le19\), cannot separate a constant change of shape from a slowly growing one. The proof attempt the review asked for first stopped where the review's Attempt did: within a parent square the circulation is one parameter and cannot change the square's average, and a martingale lower-deviation bound for adaptively shaped increments would be needed to conclude; such a bound can fail in general, since a martingale whose increment law is chosen adaptively can have light-tailed endpoints.

New mathematics. lem:per-level-gluing-fails: per-level Hall deficiencies over nested length thresholds cannot be glued into one matching at any constant factor, by a counterexample on a line (deficiency sum \(O(n)\), every matching costing about \(n^{3/2}\)). ex:greedy-circulation-tails: at \(J=6,\dots,10\) greedy circulation lowers the per-position standardized kurtosis by about \(0.2\)–\(0.3\) without a trend in \(J\), and moves variance from the first coordinate to the second.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The lemma refutes the per-level-deficiency form of the review's alternative architecture (a form with completion lengths stays queued), and the simulation finds no growing effect from the one adaptive mechanism tried. Bearing. Nothing at the goal's parameters: neither result proves or refutes a pair of the curve. The cycle was spent on them because the simulation could have found a candidate mechanism for the upper bound (a gap growing with \(J\)), and the lemma could have failed, which would have reduced the conjecture to per-level Leighton–Shor bounds.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "per-level Hall deficiency gluing perfect matching layered cost counterexample shift" and "circulation divergence-free correction square averages martingale adaptive flow" returned no related claim; tools/claim_resemblance.py on the claim spec, below the refusal threshold for both claims.

Review. The stand-in review after the last checkpoint raised five objections. (1) Queuing the review's Not run items breaks the rule that only picks join the queue: wrong; picks are the review's own queue entries, while work that comes up in a review joins the queue with lead_queue.py add, and the review's reviewer required development cycles for the Not run items before "no mechanism" could be written. (2) Three Passed items were downgraded to Not run without testing the translations: correct that they were not tested; each says why (recalled sources, simulations needed), the self-balancing one is tested here, and the others are queued (sandpile-odometer-flow, two-choice-routing). (3) A false invariance in the review draft was fixed without a prevention: correct; the review's Process assessment covers the class (statements in a decisive test's Trivial cases are checked by the staged reviewer), and no mechanical check can test such a statement. (4) The first decisive test was refuted by a shifted interval, so the Attempt was not serious: correct; the build decisive-test-structured-instance is queued in the main notebook. (5) "The level-0 term needs care" passed over an anomaly: correct, and the anomaly was misread at the time: a level of radius below \(1\) keeps the length-\(0\) edges of \(X\cap Y\), so its deficiency is \(h\), not \(n\) (this entry's reviewer); such levels add only \(O(h)\), and the lemma is now stated with them included.

Verification gate. Dependencies read: the dyadic square flow construction and the coefficient weights of the earlier entries; the review's Decisive test and its stage-two counterexample. One fresh-context medium reviewer received the draft, the script, the first run's output and log, the construction and the claim spec. It checked every step of the lemma (and showed that levels of radius below \(1\) have deficiency \(h\), so the lemma holds for every radius, now stated so), checked the script's square-flow formulas, signs, half selection and Newton step against the construction, and recomputed every quoted number. It found a discretization defect in the greedy flow (diagonal ties sent to the left and right triangles), numbers misquoted (the reference's kurtosis band missed at every size, a false comparison with the Gaussian at \(4\) standard deviations, ratios that were means), controls weaker than claimed (sign-blind; one vacuous within a square; control 1 failed as stated and was first written "confirmed"), and a wrong explanation of the reference's excess kurtosis (realizations instead of positions). The script was fixed and rerun with new expectations committed first. A follow-up with the same reviewer checked the fix, the new per-position normalization and every number of the second run, found them right, and required the corrections now made: the mixture formula's failure explained by correlated levels (its computation, unverified beyond lag one) instead of an unsupported reason, the non-blind raw prediction disclosed, the diagonal choice named, and one Nonvacuity number. Process audit. The reviewer proposed the title used, "Per-level gluing fails; greedy circulation lowers kurtosis without a growing gap", and warned against "lighter tails" or "Gaussian tails" in title or status, neither being established. It found: a control relabelled "confirmed" after it failed as pre-registered; controls described as able to catch errors they could not; a post-run explanation asserted rather than tested; the smoke run of the first round not kept; the coordinate asymmetry reported without being asked about; "closes the architecture" overstated; the stand-in answer (5) accepting a false premise and narrowing the lemma against the rule to state the general version.

Process assessment. Pre-registered controls were written without checking what they could detect, and one failure was relabelled after the run; the first round's smoke output was discarded. Prevention. The computation rules already require each control's "Refuted by" and "Possible because" lines; the gap was that "Possible because" named errors the control cannot see. A mechanical check cannot judge that; the reviewer brief now asks for it explicitly in this cycle and caught it. Smoke outputs are now saved under the run's results folder (smoke/), and the entry reports a failed control as failed, with its attributed cause.

Suggestion checked. Of the reviewer's next-attempt suggestion below (the convex optimum over all stream functions), the reduction was checked: on the simply connected square, two flows with the same divergence and the same normal flux on the boundary differ by a divergence-free field with zero normal flux, which is \(\nabla^\perp\psi\) with \(\psi\) constant on the boundary, so minimizing a convex cost over \(\psi\) vanishing on the boundary ranges over all admissible flows; the optimum is therefore the best flow for that cost, and the minimization is convex in \(\psi\). Not checked: the solver's feasibility at \(J\le9\) and the duality statement.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Replace the greedy rule by the convex optimum: \(\Phi=\)dyadic\(+\nabla^\perp\psi\) over all discrete stream functions vanishing on the boundary, minimizing \(\sum_x|\Phi(x)|^p\) for \(p=8\) or \(16\) (or the exponential cost) by a preconditioned gradient method at \(J\le9\); its per-point law is the best any flow achieves for that cost, so its tails decide the flow route at reachable sizes. Record. The search "convex optimal flow stream function minimize Orlicz cost divergence constraint duality" returned nothing related; lem:flow-matching-bound bears on it.
  • Within-square rules may provably keep square averages Gaussian: circulations at levels at or below a square's level add no flux across its boundary, so its average depends only on the dyadic field and its strict ancestors' circulations, and by Jensen the square averages bound the cost from below; the missing piece is control of the ancestors' boundary terms. Record. thm:dyadic-flow-obstruction and lem:block-lower-tail bear on it; the search "circulation divergence-free correction square averages martingale adaptive flow" returned nothing more.
  • The variance shift between the coordinates would vanish if the quartic weighted each coordinate by its inverse dyadic variance; a rerun with that weighting tests the entry's explanation. Record. No registered claim concerns it (same search).

Follow-up. Continuing. The greedy circulation, one free scalar per square with a fixed pyramid profile, gave a constant change of shape and no growing gap; the item still bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy through its primal form. Next attempt: the convex optimum over all stream functions, minimizing \(\sum_x|\Phi(x)|^8\) at \(J\le9\), whose per-point tails are the best any flow achieves for that cost; if they stay near Gaussian with no growing gap, the flow route at reachable sizes has no adaptive mechanism to offer, and the next review weighs the route against the queued refutation tests.

Convex-optimal flows: a run that could not decide, stopped

16 October 2026 · No result; computation stopped · Convex flow test. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Best outcome. At the goal's own pair \((4,4)\): numerical evidence, at reachable sizes, that the best flow for an \(L^8\) proxy keeps the flow bound's \((4,4)\) cost bounded at a fixed constant while the dyadic flow's needs a growing one, which would support conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\) without proving anything. That was the most it could give.

Chosen. The item entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3 continues from the adaptive circulation test ("gave a constant change of shape and no growing gap"), whose follow-up named this attempt; its earlier developments, the Hopf–Lax reduction ("The reduction is proved, including the endpoints and the comparison with weights at least linear"), the Leighton–Shor class test ("The reduced statement is not refuted by its first test"), the dyadic flow bound ("The item now has a primal criterion and a calibration") and the dyadic flow obstruction ("The item reaches its spell boundary with this entry and rotates"), were read in this session. It bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at the goal's pair \((4,4)\).

Owed attempt. The latest follow-up named "the convex optimum over all stream functions, minimizing \(\sum_x|\Phi(x)|^8\) at \(J\le9\)". Made in part and stopped: research/results/ultimate_matching_convex_flow_20261016/convex_flow_test.py (expectations committed before the run; smoke output under smoke/) computed the optimal flows for \(p=2,4,8\) and the least constant \(L\) at which the \((4,4)\) flow-bound cost stays below \(1/2\); the run finished \(J=5,6,7\) with two seeds each and was stopped by the user during \(J=8\) (saved output convex_flow_results.json).

General statement. The run probed conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\) through its primal form; it registers no claim.

What the finished cases show. Least \(L\) for the \(p=8\) optimum, coordinates \(1\) and \(2\): \(J=5\): \(1.15, 1.19\) and \(1.33, 1.17\); \(J=6\): \(1.23, 1.24\) and \(1.39, 1.42\); \(J=7\): \(1.36, 1.31\) and \(1.25, 1.19\). For the dyadic flow: \(2.28, 1.96\) and \(2.72, 1.46\); \(2.86, 1.82\) and \(2.95, 1.65\); \(3.07, 2.04\) and \(2.95, 1.80\). The optimum needs a constant about half the dyadic flow's, and its per-point kurtosis is \(1.7\)–\(2.2\) in most cases (far lighter than Gaussian). The spread between the two seeds at one size (up to \(0.18\)) is as large as any change from \(J=5\) to \(J=7\), and the \(p=8\) optimization stopped at its iteration cap with gradient ratios up to \(9.5\times10^{-5}\), above the control's \(10^{-5}\), at \(J\ge6\).

Why it could not decide. Whether the optimal flow's constant stays bounded as \(N\) grows is, up to the open converse of the Hopf–Lax bound, the reduced conjecture itself at \((4,4)\); its two outcomes differ by a power of \(\log N\), and over \(\log N\) from \(10\) to \(14\) such a power changes by a few percent, less than the spread between seeds. No size reachable here separates them. The run was also serial in one process, although its seeds and sizes were independent.

New mathematics. None. The finished cases show only that light-tailed flows exist at these sizes (the optimum's kurtosis well below \(3\), its needed constant about half the dyadic flow's), which the route assumed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. Bearing. Nothing at the goal's parameters: the run could not separate a bounded constant from one growing like a power of \(\log N\). It was spent because it could have shown the optimal flow's constant growing as fast as the dyadic flow's, a sign against the flow route at \((4,4)\); it did not, and could not have shown the opposite.

(5) Spec values and short answers were fixed one at a time: claim_resemblance.py now reports spec values outside the registry schema when the spec is first drafted (committed the same day); short answers are refused by the append tool, which is that check.

Maximal displacements: exact threshold at the curve, by Leighton–Shor on elongated rectangles

16 October 2026 · Working proof; maximum-displacement consequence only · Maximum-displacement threshold. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Best outcome. At the goal's own parameters: a proof that the maximum-displacement consequence of the conjecture holds at every pair of the curve, which is one of its two consequences (lem:exponential-matching-consequences), and that no argument using only maximal displacements can refute a pair of the curve.

Chosen. The queue head entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s2 (reading the paper [C5]) waits on a source the user was asked for in user_requests (ResearchGate refuses scripted access, no other copy was found, and no signed-in session is saved). This entry takes the queued check max-displacement-curve-barrier instead, which bears on ultimate-matching directly: it asks whether the reviewer's conditional barrier on refutations holds. The user supplied [C5] during the cycle; it is the next entry's reading.

Owed attempt. The item has no earlier development; it names its own question, the Leighton–Shor upper bound on rectangles of aspect ratio up to \((\log N)^{1/2}\), answered below.

General statement. thm:maximum-displacement-curve-threshold: for every exponent pair in \((0,\infty]^2\), the maximum-displacement form of the conjecture holds, with one universal constant and probability tending to one, exactly on and above the curve \(1/\alpha_1+1/\alpha_2=1/2\), in both settings; below the curve it fails for every constant.

Anisotropic Leighton–Shor bound

Sources read. [T4] Theorem 4.7.1 (statement and probability \(1-L\exp(-(\log N)^{3/2}/L)\)), Theorem 4.7.2 (the discrepancy bound (4.103) over all closed simple curves traced on the grid of mesh \(2^{-\ell_1}\), \(2^{-\ell_1}\ge(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\)), the chord form (B.10) of that bound and the proof of Theorem 4.7.1 in Appendix B.3, (B.10)–(B.21): "if (4.103) and (B.10) hold, and provided \(\ell_2\) has been chosen appropriately, then for any choice of \(R\) ... (B.11). We will then conclude with Hall's Marriage Lemma"; (B.10) is the same discrepancy bound for chords. The definition of evenly spread points is the one recorded in the working context from the monograph reading (cells of area \(1/N\), each inside \(B(Y_i,10/\sqrt N)\)). The quantile step uses Hoeffding's inequality only.

Why a lemma is needed. Leighton–Shor on the unit square gives a maximum \(L(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\) in both coordinates, which is the maximum form at a pair only when \(\beta_1,\beta_2\ge1/4\): on the curve only at \((4,4)\). Rescaling Theorem 4.7.1 to a rectangle of aspect \(a/b\) by an affine map multiplies the long side's displacement by \(\sqrt{a/b}\) more than the target \(\sqrt{ab/N}(\log N)^{3/4}\) allows. The lemma below removes that factor.

Statement. There are universal \(K\) and \(N_0\) such that for \(N\ge N_0\) and every rectangle \(Q=[0,a]\times[0,b]\) with \(1\le a/b\le(\log N)^{1/2}\) there is a set \(Z\subset Q\) of \(N\) points, whose cells tile \(Q\) by sets of area \(ab/N\) and diameter at most \(K\sqrt{ab/N}\), such that if \(X_1,\dots,X_N\) are i.i.d. uniform on \(Q\), then with probability at least \(1-2(\log N)^{1/2}N^{-2}-L(\log N)^{1/2}\exp(-(\log N)^{3/2}/(3L))\) some matching \(\pi\) has \(|X_i^j-Z^j_{\pi(i)}|\le K\sqrt{ab/N}\,(\log N)^{3/4}\) for every \(i\) and \(j=1,2\).

Proof. Strips. Let \(M=\lfloor a/b\rfloor\in[1,(\log N)^{1/2}]\), \(m_k=\lfloor kN/M\rfloor\), \(n_k=m_k-m_{k-1}\ge N/M-1\), \(p_k=am_k/N\), and \(S_k=[p_{k-1},p_k]\times[0,b]\), of width \(w_k=an_k/N\in[b/2,2b]\) for large \(N\) (since \(a/M\in[b,2b)\)). Let \(A_k\) be the affine map of \(S_k\) onto \([0,1]^2\) scaling the first coordinate by \(1/w_k\) and the second by \(1/b\), let \(W_k\) be an evenly spread set of \(n_k\) points of \([0,1]^2\) in [T4]'s sense, and put \(Z=\bigcup_kA_k^{-1}(W_k)\). Its cells, the preimages of \(W_k\)'s rectangles, tile \(Q\), have area \(w_kb/n_k=ab/N\) and diameter at most \(10\max(w_k,b)/\sqrt{n_k}\le K\sqrt{ab/N}\), since \(\max(w_k,b)\le2b\) and \(b^2M\le ab\).

Quantile shift. Order the \(X_i\) by first coordinate (almost surely distinct) and let \(c_k\) be the first coordinate of the point of rank \(m_k\) (\(c_0=0\), \(c_M=a\)). Let \(\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log N/N}\). Since \(p_k/a=m_k/N\), \(c_k>p_k+a\varepsilon\) means that fewer than \(m_k\) first coordinates lie below \(p_k+a\varepsilon\), a binomial count of mean \(m_k+N\varepsilon\) falling \(N\varepsilon\) below its mean, of probability at most \(e^{-2N\varepsilon^2}=N^{-2}\) by Hoeffding's inequality; \(c_k<p_k-a\varepsilon\) is bounded in the same way. Over the \(M-1\) inner quantiles, \(|c_k-p_k|\le a\varepsilon\) for every \(k\) with probability at least \(1-2MN^{-2}\). Map the quantile strip \([c_{k-1},c_k]\times[0,b]\) onto \(S_k\) by the affine map \(T_k\) of the first coordinate; the points of ranks \(m_{k-1}+1,\dots,m_k\) go to \(S_k\), each moved horizontally by at most \(\max(|c_{k-1}-p_{k-1}|,|c_k-p_k|)\le a\sqrt{\log N/N}\). Given \(c_{k-1},c_k\), the points of ranks strictly between \(m_{k-1}\) and \(m_k\) are i.i.d. uniform on the quantile strip (the classical conditional law of the order statistics, the second coordinates being independent of the first), so their images are i.i.d. uniform on \(S_k\); the point of rank \(m_k\) (for \(k<M\)) lands on the right edge of \(S_k\). So \(A_k\circ T_k\) sends strip \(k\)'s points to \(n_k-1\) i.i.d. uniform points of \([0,1]^2\) and at most one further point, on the edge \(\{1\}\times[0,1]\). That edge lies on every grid of Appendix B.3, whose deduction assumes no point on the grid; so move the further point horizontally into the open square it borders, by less than the mesh \(2^{-\ell_2}\) of the grid \(G'\) below (its second coordinate lies on no grid line, almost surely). This adds at most \(w_k2^{-\ell_2}\le2bK'(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt{n_k}\) (\(K'\) universal, \(\ell_2\) being the largest level allowed by (B.19)) to its displacement after pulling back, within the bound of the next step.

Matching in each strip. The deterministic part of Theorem 4.7.1's proof (Appendix B.3) uses the points only through the events (4.103) and (B.10), and only for curves and chords traced on a coarser grid \(G'\) of mesh \(2^{-\ell_2}\) chosen by (B.19), \(2^{-\ell_2}\ge2^6L(\log n)^{3/4}/\sqrt n\), with the evenly spread points entering through (B.20)–(B.21). For the \(n-1\) i.i.d. points, Theorem 4.7.2 and its chord form (B.10) give these events with probability at least \(1-L\exp(-(\log(n-1))^{3/2}/L)\) on the grid of mesh \(2^{-\ell_1(n-1)}\), which contains \(G'\) (its mesh \(2^{-\ell_1(n-1)}\le2\cdot2^{-\ell_1(n)}\) is finer than \(2^{-\ell_2}\)). Both bound \(|\sum_i1_V(X_i)-n\lambda(V)|\) by \(L\ell\sqrt n(\log n)^{3/4}\), and on \(G'\) every curve or chord has \(\ell\ge2^{-\ell_2}\), so the right side is at least \(2^6L^2(\log n)^{3/2}\ge2\). Adding one point off the grid and normalizing by \(n\) instead of \(n-1\) changes each left side by at most \(1+\lambda(V)\le2\) (and \(\sqrt{n-1}\) by a factor at most \(1\)), so both events hold for the \(n\)-point set with \(2L\) in place of \(L\); with \(2L\), (B.19) asks a grid coarser by a factor \(2\), and Theorem 4.7.1's conclusion holds for the \(n\)-point set with a larger universal constant \(L'\). So, outside an event of probability at most \(L\exp(-(\log(n_k-1))^{3/2}/L)\), the images of strip \(k\)'s points match \(W_k\) within distance \(L'(\log n_k)^{3/4}/\sqrt{n_k}\); pulled back by \(A_k^{-1}\), the matching of strip \(k\)'s mapped points to \(A_k^{-1}(W_k)\) moves each coordinate by at most \(2bL'(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt{n_k}\le K\sqrt{ab/N}(\log N)^{3/4}\), using \(n_k\ge N/(2M)\) and \(b\sqrt M\le\sqrt{ab}\). A union bound over the \(M\le(\log N)^{1/2}\) strips, with \(n_k-1\ge N^{1/2}\) for large \(N\), gives the failure probability stated.

Total. Composing, \(|X_i^1-Z^1_{\pi(i)}|\le a\sqrt{\log N/N}+K\sqrt{ab/N}(\log N)^{3/4}\) and \(|X_i^2-Z^2_{\pi(i)}|\le K\sqrt{ab/N}(\log N)^{3/4}\). Finally \(a\sqrt{\log N/N}\le\sqrt{ab/N}(\log N)^{3/4}\) exactly when \(a/b\le(\log N)^{1/2}\). \(\square\)

Where the aspect bound enters. In the quantile shift: equalizing the strips' counts moves points horizontally by about \(a\sqrt{\log N/N}\), which fits the Leighton–Shor scale \(\sqrt{ab/N}(\log N)^{3/4}\) up to aspect ratio \((\log N)^{1/2}\) and exceeds it beyond; and, mildly, in the union bound over \(M\le(\log N)^{1/2}\) strips and in \(n_k-1\ge N^{1/2}\). The bound \((\log N)^{1/2}\) is this method's limit, not a proved threshold: the typical fluctuation of a strip's count is of order \(\sqrt{N/M}\), and a horizontal shift of that order costs about \(a/\sqrt{NM}\), which stays below the target up to aspect \((\log N)^{3/2}\); whether the lemma extends that far is not decided here.

Maximum-displacement threshold

Statement. Let \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in(0,\infty]\), \(\beta_j=1/\alpha_j\), \(u_j=(\log N)^{\beta_j}\), \(s_1=\sqrt{\log N/N}\). (i) If \(\beta_1+\beta_2\ge1/2\), there is a universal \(K\) such that, for \(X\) i.i.d. uniform on \([0,1]^2\) and \(Y\) evenly spread or an independent uniform sample, with probability tending to one some matching has \(\max_i|X_i^j-Y^j_{\pi(i)}|\le Ku_js_1\) for \(j=1,2\). (ii) If \(\beta_1+\beta_2<1/2\), for every \(K\), with probability tending to one no matching has this property.

Proof. (i) First let \(\beta_1,\beta_2\le1/2\); by symmetry \(\beta_1\ge\beta_2\). Rescale \(x'_j=x_j/u_j\): the square becomes \(Q=[0,1/u_1]\times[0,1/u_2]\), with sides \(a=1/u_2\ge b=1/u_1\) (the lemma with the coordinates exchanged), aspect \(u_1/u_2=(\log N)^{\beta_1-\beta_2}\le(\log N)^{1/2}\) since \(0\le\beta_j\le1/2\), area \((\log N)^{-\beta_1-\beta_2}\), and \(X'\) i.i.d. uniform on \(Q\). The lemma matches \(X'\) to \(Z\) within \(K(\log N)^{3/4-(\beta_1+\beta_2)/2}/\sqrt N\le K(\log N)^{1/2}/\sqrt N\) in each coordinate; back in the square, coordinate \(j\) moves by at most \(Ku_js_1\). The image of \(Z\) in the square has cells of area \(1/N\) tiling the square, of extent at most \(Ku_j(\log N)^{-(\beta_1+\beta_2)/2}/\sqrt N\le Ku_j/\sqrt N\) in coordinate \(j\). Evenly spread \(Y\): for any set \(U\) of points of \(Y\), the cells of \(Z\) meeting the union of \(U\)'s cells cover that union, which has area \(|U|/N\), so there are at least \(|U|\) of them, each holding a point of \(Z\) within \(10/\sqrt N+Ku_j/\sqrt N\) in coordinate \(j\) of a point of \(U\); Hall's theorem matches \(Y\) to \(Z\) within that distance, and composing gives (i) with a larger \(K\). Two samples: apply the lemma to \(Y\) as well and compose through \(Z\) (as in lem:two-sample-matching-transfer at exponent \(\infty\)). In general put \(\beta'_j=\min(\beta_j,1/2)\): then \(\beta'_1+\beta'_2\ge1/2\) still (if a \(\beta_j\) was capped, \(\beta'_j=1/2\) alone suffices), the case just proved gives a matching within \(K(\log N)^{\beta'_j}s_1\le Ku_js_1\), and so (i) holds at \(\beta\). (ii) The proof of thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion uses of a good matching only that \(|\Delta_i^j|\le su_j\) for all but at most two pairs (Step 3), with \(s=L s_1\); a matching with \(\max_i|\Delta_i^j|\le Ku_js_1\) satisfies this with \(L=K\) and no exception, so the same proof excludes it, for every \(K\). \(\square\)

What it gives. By lem:exponential-matching-consequences, a good matching at a pair of the curve has \(\max_i|\Delta_i^j|\le L\sqrt{\log N}(\log2N)^{\beta_j}/\sqrt N\); part (i) proves this consequence of the conjecture, with a universal constant, at every pair of the curve. It also settles the queued barrier: an argument that uses of a good matching only its maximal displacements cannot refute any pair of the curve, since such matchings exist there; a refutation would have to use more of the condition than maximal displacements (the counts at intermediate levels, or their coupling within one matching). At \((4,4)\) part (i) is Leighton–Shor's theorem; at \((2,\infty)\) it gives a matching with vertical displacements at Shor's scale \(\sqrt{\log N/N}\) and horizontal ones at most \(K\log N/\sqrt N\).

Degenerate cases. Lemma: \(a=b\) is [T4]'s Theorem 4.7.1 itself (\(M=1\), no quantile shift); \(N<N_0\) is excluded. Theorem: \(\alpha_1=\alpha_2=\infty\) lies below the curve, and (ii) gives that no matching has both displacements at \(Ks_1\), consistent with Leighton–Shor's lower bound. An exponent below \(2\) is covered by capping \(\beta_j\) at \(1/2\), so the lemma is never applied beyond aspect \((\log N)^{1/2}\); \(\alpha_1=\alpha_2\) tiny gives an allowance above the diameter of the square, where (i) is trivial.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters: the pair \((3,6)\), \(\beta_1=1/3\), \(\beta_2=1/6\), \(u_1=(\log N)^{1/3}\), \(u_2=(\log N)^{1/6}\), aspect \((\log N)^{1/6}\le(\log N)^{1/2}\), area \((\log N)^{-1/2}\); the lemma's displacement there is \(K(\log N)^{1/2}/\sqrt N\).

Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "Leighton Shor rectangle aspect ratio anisotropic maximum displacement matching quantile strips" returned thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion, lem:common-exponent-cap and lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, all lower bounds; the search returned no upper bound on rectangles.

New mathematics. The Leighton–Shor upper bound extends to rectangles of aspect ratio up to \((\log N)^{1/2}\) by quantile strips, the strips' single boundary points being absorbed by the slack in [T4]'s discrepancy events. Consequently the maximum-displacement form of the conjecture holds exactly on and above the curve and fails below it.

Goal progress. Unchanged: the maximum-displacement form is a consequence of the conjecture, not the conjecture; the exponential moments at intermediate levels remain open. Bearing. At every pair of the curve's parameters the theorem proves the conjecture's maximum consequence with a universal constant, and it shows that a refutation must use more of the condition than maximal displacements. Not covered. The intermediate levels: the bound on the number of pairs with \(|\Delta_i^j|>s\,t^{1/\alpha_j}\) for \(1\ll t\ll\log N\), where the conjecture asks at most \(2Ne^{-t}\) and the theorem gives nothing.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • A per-level construction: at level \(t\) rescale coordinate \(j\) by \(t^{\beta_j}\), tile by squares holding about \(e^{2t}\) expected points, and match by Leighton–Shor inside each square; the count mismatch would leave about \(Ne^{-t}\) points unmatched, so every single-level count consequence of the conjecture would hold on the curve, and a refutation would have to use the coupling of levels within one matching. Boxes of area \(s^2\sqrt t\) cannot do this (count fluctuations exceed \(2Ne^{-t}\) once \(t\gtrsim\frac12\log\log N\)), and gluing levels is blocked by lem:per-level-gluing-fails. Record. tools/search-claims.py for "per-level count matching squares Leighton Shor level t rescaled each coordinate deficiency" returned lem:per-level-gluing-fails (the gluing obstruction it cites), lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion and thm:dyadic-flow-obstruction; no per-level upper bound is on record.
  • Whether the aspect limit \((\log N)^{1/2}\) is the truth or the method: count fluctuations alone allow aspect up to \((\log N)^{3/2}\). Record. tools/search-claims.py for "Hoeffding quantile strips equal counts aspect ratio" returned nothing on rectangles; not needed for the theorem, which never exceeds aspect \((\log N)^{1/2}\).

Position-blind cell exits fail; [C5] fails \((4,4)\)

16 October 2026 · Working proof; each fixed position-blind rule · Position-blind cell exits. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Owed attempt. The item has no earlier development; its own question, whether the construction of [C5] gives the exponential moments of \((4,4)\), is answered below.

Sources read. [C5] (F. Ouimet, with ChatGPT; "A solution to Problem 5.3 in Talagrand's My favorite problems: Simultaneous sum and bottleneck bounds for random planar matchings", doi:10.13140/RG.2.2.26527.93606/2; supplied by the user, held privately), read in its statements and construction: Theorem 2.1 (with high probability some matching has sum \(\le L\sqrt{N\log N}\) and maximum \(\le L(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\)), the parameters (2.2)–(2.3) (cells \(Q_u\) of side \(s\) with \(K(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\le s\le2K(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\), \(\lambda=Ns^2\)), Lemmas 2.4, 2.5, 2.10 and 2.11 with their proofs, Section 2.5 (local matchings: "Select all points from the smaller of the two samples in \(Q_u\) and, when the sample counts are unequal, the \(k_u\) points with the smallest original labels from the larger sample ... The remaining \(|z(u)|\) points ... will be called the excess points of \(Q_u\)") and Section 2.6 (conversion of the integral cell flow into a matching: each unit path \(u_0,\dots,u_t\), \(t\ge1\), pairs an excess \(X\) point of \(Q_{u_0}\) with a point of \(Q_{u_1}\)). The proofs of Lemmas 2.6–2.9 (electrical energy, maximal inequalities, Lipschitz truncation) were not read: they bound the flow, and nothing below uses them.

General statement. thm:position-blind-cell-exits: for every exponent pair, every constant and each fixed rule, a matching that sends a label-chosen excess of each square cell of side at least \((\log N)^{1/2+\gamma}/\sqrt N\) out of its cell fails the exponential condition with probability tending to one; [C5]'s matching is one, at \((4,4)\).

Level-count consequence

Statement. If \(\pi\) is \((L,\alpha)\)-good in coordinate \(j\) with \(\alpha\in(0,\infty)\), then for every \(t\ge0\), \(\#\{i:|X_i^j-Y^j_{\pi(i)}|\ge L\sqrt{\log N/N}\,t^{1/\alpha}\}\le2Ne^{-t}\). Consequently, for every \(a,\delta,\beta>0\), if more than \(N(\log N)^{-a}\) pairs have \(|X_i^j-Y^j_{\pi(i)}|\ge\delta(\log N)^{1/2+\beta}/\sqrt N\), then \(\pi\) is not \((L,\alpha)\)-good in coordinate \(j\) once \(N\ge N_0(a,\delta,\beta,L,\alpha)\).

Proof. Each pair counted has \(\exp[(|\Delta_i^j|/(L\sqrt{\log N/N}))^\alpha]\ge e^t\), and the terms are positive with sum at most \(2N\). For the consequence take \(t=(\delta/L)^\alpha(\log N)^{\alpha\beta}\): the bound \(2Ne^{-t}\) is below \(N(\log N)^{-a}\) once \(t>a\log\log N+\log2\), which holds for large \(N\) since \(\alpha\beta>0\). \(\square\)

Position-blind cell exits

Setting. \(X_1,\dots,X_N\), \(Y_1,\dots,Y_N\) independent and uniform on \([0,1]^2\), each with its index as label; \([0,1]^2\) is cut into \(m^2\) congruent half-open squares \(Q_u\) of side \(s=1/m\), \(\lambda=Ns^2\); \(z(u)\) is the number of \(X\) points in \(Q_u\) minus the number of \(Y\) points. A position-blind exit rule (one for each \(N\), measurable) chooses, in each \(Q_u\) with \(z(u)>0\), a set \(E_u\) of \(z(u)\) of its \(X\) points by a rule that depends only on which cell each point of both samples lies in and on the labels, and produces, possibly only on an event of probability tending to one, a matching in which every point of every \(E_u\) is matched to a \(Y\) point outside \(Q_u\).

Statement. Let \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in(0,\infty]\), and let \(s=s_N\to0\) satisfy \(s\sqrt N\ge\delta(\log N)^{1/2+\gamma}\) for fixed \(\delta,\gamma>0\). For every fixed position-blind exit rule and every \(L>0\), with probability tending to one the matching it produces fails to be \((L,\alpha_j)\)-good in some coordinate \(j\). In particular the matching of [C5] (\(s\sqrt N\in[K(\log N)^{3/4},2K(\log N)^{3/4}]\), its excess points chosen by largest labels and each paired with a point of a side-neighbouring cell) fails the \((4,4)\) condition for every \(L\).

Proof. Many exits. Let \(f=\sum_u|z(u)|\). As a function of the \(2N\) independent points, \(f\) changes by at most \(2\) when one point moves, so by McDiarmid's inequality \(\Pr(f<\mathbb Ef-r)\le\exp(-r^2/(4N))\). Each \(z(u)\) is a difference of two independent binomial\((N,1/m^2)\) variables, of variance \(\sigma^2=2N m^{-2}(1-m^{-2})\) and fourth moment at most \(3\sigma^4+2\sigma^2\) (each binomial's fourth cumulant is at most its mean, and \(m^{-2}\le1/2\)); by Hölder, \(\mathbb Ez^2\le(\mathbb E|z|)^{2/3}(\mathbb Ez^4)^{1/3}\), so \(\mathbb E|z|\ge\sigma/\sqrt{3+2\sigma^{-2}}\ge0.5\sqrt\lambda\) for large \(\lambda\), as \(\sigma^2\ge\lambda\). So \(\mathbb Ef\ge0.5\,m^2\sqrt\lambda=0.5N/\sqrt\lambda\), and with \(r=0.25N/\sqrt\lambda\), \(f\ge0.25N/\sqrt\lambda\) outside probability \(\exp(-N/(64\lambda))=\exp(-m^2/64)\to0\), since \(s\to0\); here \(\lambda=Ns^2\ge\delta^2(\log N)^{1+2\gamma}\to\infty\). As \(\sum_uz(u)=0\), the exits number \(\sum_u\max(z(u),0)=f/2\ge N/(8\sqrt\lambda)\).

Deep exits. Condition on the cell of every point and on the labels. The positions within the cells are then independent and uniform, and the sets \(E_u\) are fixed, so the exiting points are independent and uniform in their cells. Each lies in the central square of its cell (side \(s/2\), at sup-distance at least \(s/4\) from the complement of the cell) with probability \(1/4\); by Chernoff's bound, at least \(N/(64\sqrt\lambda)\) exits are central, outside a probability tending to zero. A central exiting point \(x\in Q_u\) matched to \(y\notin Q_u\) has \(|x^j-y^j|\ge s/4\) in some coordinate \(j\); so for one coordinate \(j\), at least \(N/(128\sqrt\lambda)=\sqrt N/(128s)\) pairs have \(|\Delta_i^j|\ge s/4\).

Conclusion. Put \(x=s\sqrt N/\sqrt{\log N}\ge\delta(\log N)^\gamma\to\infty\), so that \(s/4=(x/4)\sqrt{\log N/N}\). If \(\alpha_j=\infty\), goodness asks \(|\Delta_i^j|\le L\sqrt{\log N/N}\), which these pairs violate once \(x>4L\). If \(\alpha_j<\infty\), the level count (the first part of the lemma) at \(t=(x/(4L))^{\alpha_j}\) allows at most \(2Ne^{-t}\) such pairs, fewer than \(\sqrt N/(128s)\) once \(t>\log(256s\sqrt N)=\log(256x)+\frac12\log\log N\); as \(\log\log N\le\gamma^{-1}\log(x/\delta)\), the right side is \(O(\log x)\) while \(t\) is a power of \(x\), so this holds for large \(N\). For [C5]: its Section 2.5 chooses the excess as the points of largest labels, which depends on cell memberships and labels only, and its Section 2.6 pairs each excess \(X\) point of \(Q_{u_0}\) with a point of the cell \(Q_{u_1}\ne Q_{u_0}\), on events of probability tending to one; \(\gamma=1/4\), \(\delta=K\). \(\square\)

What it shows. Any matching moves at least \(f/2\) points out of their cells; what fails is choosing them blind to position. A necessary condition follows from the level counts: at \((4,4)\), all but \(4Ne^{-t}\) of the points that leave a cell (\(2Ne^{-t}\) for each coordinate) lie within \(L\sqrt{\log N/N}\,t^{1/4}\) of its boundary, so a construction has to select each cell's exits by position. The maximum-displacement threshold showed that maximal displacements alone refute no pair of the curve; this obstruction uses the level counts, and excludes a class of constructions, not a pair. [C5] answers Problem 5.3 (both bounds at once) inside this class, in line with the survey's remark that its solution "does the job, but does not seem to make progress on the real issues" (the survey's Section 5, read in the monograph reading and re-read this session).

Degenerate cases. \(\alpha_1=\alpha_2=\infty\) is covered: there the pairs moving \(s/4\gg\sqrt{\log N/N}\) violate the maximum. An empty excess (\(z\equiv0\)) has probability tending to zero. A rule choosing exits by position (nearest the side) is not position-blind and is not covered. Cells of fixed size (\(s\not\to0\)) are excluded by hypothesis: the proof's concentration step needs \(m\to\infty\); the reviewer notes that a limit-law argument would cover them too, which is not done here.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters: the pair \((4,4)\) and [C5]'s construction with \(s\sqrt N=K(\log N)^{3/4}\), \(\lambda=K^2(\log N)^{3/2}\), \(\gamma=1/4\), which its Lemma 2.4 and Section 2.6 show defined with probability tending to one.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "number of pairs displacement exceeds level count Markov exponential moment good matching tail" returned no count bound (lem:exponential-matching-consequences states the maximum and the sum only); for "excess points cell matched outside uniform position coarse grid flow matching" it returned lem:anisotropic-leighton-shor-bound, ex:greedy-circulation-tails and thm:dyadic-flow-obstruction, none about exits.

New mathematics. A level-count form of the exponential condition, and an obstruction: for each fixed rule choosing cell exits blind to position, on cells of side at least \((\log N)^{1/2+\gamma}/\sqrt N\), the matching fails the condition for every pair and every constant; [C5]'s solution of Problem 5.3 is such a matching, so it fails \((4,4)\).

Goal progress. Unchanged: a known construction is excluded, and a class of constructions with it, but no matching is built. Bearing. At the goal's pair \((4,4)\) on the curve's parameters, each fixed position-blind exit scheme on cells of side at least \(\delta(\log N)^{1/2+\gamma}/\sqrt N\), [C5]'s included, fails; a construction for \((4,4)\) must choose which points leave each coarse cell by their positions. Not covered. Position-aware exits, cells finer than \((\log N)^{1/2+\gamma}/\sqrt N\) for every \(\gamma>0\), and constructions with no cell structure.

(1) A test module was run outside compute.sh with no prevention stated in the reply: right; bash_guard now refuses a test module run as a program (committed the same hour, with a test).
