Leighton–Shor bands at several aspects overlap with \(o(1)\) penalty

16 October 2026 · Working proof; vertically scaled, positively oriented members · Overlap of Leighton–Shor bands. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Chosen. The queue head is entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3. This entry takes leighton-shor-overlap-penalty first: it bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy through the second route review's decisive test, which the aligned superposition bound left Not decided for the posed object, and one computation decides it.

Owed attempt. The item has no earlier development; its queued text names the attempt: bound where the bands of two members meet by \(w\) times the crossings of their graphs, and the members' horizontal penalty there. Made, below, in a sharper form that counts linear pieces of the difference of the two graphs, not crossings.

Question. The scaled Leighton–Shor test functions at several aspects, built on the whole square, are not aligned: their horizontal gradients take both signs where their bands overlap. Does their misaligned overlap carry penalty \(\sigma=o(1)\)? If so, the aligned superposition bound applies to the review's superposition, and the architecture test is decided in the class form.

General statement. lem:band-overlap-penalty, for every family of band functions over piecewise linear graphs and every convex penalty that is at most linear with slope \(\rho\) on the relevant range.

Band overlap penalty

Setting. \(\Omega=[0,1]^2\). For continuous piecewise linear \(f:[0,1]\to\mathbb R\), \(w>0\) and \(A>0\), the band function is \(G(x,y)=A\min\{1,\max\{0,(f(x)+w-y)/w\}\}\), as in Step 2 of lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound; its gradient vanishes almost everywhere outside the band \(B=\{(x,y)\in\Omega:f(x)<y<f(x)+w\}\), and on \(B\) it is \((Af'(x)/w,-A/w)\). For band functions \(G_1,\dots,G_m\) (graphs \(f_k\), amplitudes \(A_k\), common \(w\)) and penalties \(\varphi_1,\varphi_2\ge0\) (convexity is used only in (3)), let \(E_j\) and \(\sigma\) be as in lem:aligned-superposition-bound (the misaligned sets and the members' penalty on them).

Statement. (1) Suppose \(\varphi_1(y)\le\rho y\) for \(0\le y\le Y\), \(A_k|f_k'|\le wY\) almost everywhere, and every difference \(f_k-f_{k'}\), \(k\ne k'\), has at most \(P\) linear pieces. Then \(E_2\) is null and \[\sigma\le2\rho wP(m-1)\sum_kA_k.\] (2) Let \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in[1,\infty)\), \(\varphi_j=\psi^*_{\alpha_j}\), and let \(f_k=\kappa_kF_k\) with \(F_k\) of the form (4.122) of [T4] (\(F_k=\sum_{q\le r}\sum_\ell x_{q,\ell}f_{cq,\ell}/\sqrt r\), \(x_{q,\ell}\in\{0,1\}\), \(c\) and \(r\) as in Step 1 of lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound and common to all members, so \(2^{cr}\le N^{1/100}\) and \(r\le\log N\)), with aspects \(\kappa_k\in[(\log N)^{-1/2},(\log N)^{1/2}]\), amplitudes \(A_k\le1\) and \(w=\delta\), the grid side of cor:exponential-matching-discrepancy-reduction. Then for \(N\) large \[\sigma\le\frac{L}{4}\,m^2(\log N)^{3/2}N^{-1/2+1/100}.\] (3) In the setting of (2), with \(m\le(\log N)^C\), \(\Phi(\sum_kG_k)\le2\) and \(\ell=\int\cdot\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)\): \[\ell\Big(\sum_kG_k\Big)\le\Big(1+\frac\sigma2\Big)\Big(1+c_0\frac{\log\log N}{\log N}\Big)\max_k\ell(H_k)^+,\] for \(N\ge N_0(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,C,L)\), where \(H_k\) is \(G_k\) rescaled to \(\Phi(H_k)=2\) and \(c_0\) depends on \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,C\) only. So a superposition of scaled Leighton–Shor band functions (all with \(+\) sign, all vertically scaled) at any \(m\le(\log N)^C\) aspects is worth at most \(1+o(1)\) times its best member at full budget, with no additive error.

Proof. (1) Every \(\partial_2G_k\) is \(-A_k/w\) or \(0\), so \(E_2\) is null. Fix \(k\). At a point of \(E_1\) where \(\partial_1G_k\ne0\), some \(k'\ne k\) has \(\partial_1G_{k'}\) nonzero of the opposite sign, so the point lies in \(B_k\cap B_{k'}\) and \(f_k'(x)f_{k'}'(x)<0\); and \(B_k\cap B_{k'}\) meets the vertical line at \(x\) only if \(|f_k(x)-f_{k'}(x)|<w\), in a segment of length at most \(w\). With \(T_{kk'}=\{x:|h(x)|<w,\ f_k'f_{k'}'<0\}\), \(h=f_k-f_{k'}\), member \(k\)'s penalty on \(E_1\) is at most \[\sum_{k'\ne k}\int_{T_{kk'}}w\,\varphi_1\Big(\frac{A_k|f_k'|}{w}\Big)dx\le\rho A_k\sum_{k'\ne k}\int_{T_{kk'}}|f_k'|\,dx\le\rho A_k\sum_{k'\ne k}\int_{\{|h|<w\}}|h'|\,dx,\] using \(\varphi_1(y)\le\rho y\) for \(y=A_k|f_k'|/w\le Y\), and, on \(T_{kk'}\), \(|h'|=|f_k'|+|f_{k'}'|\ge|f_k'|\) since the two slopes have opposite signs. On a linear piece of \(h\) with slope \(s\ne0\) the set \(\{|h|<w\}\) is an interval of length at most \(2w/|s|\), on which \(\int|h'|\le2w\); pieces with \(s=0\) contribute \(0\). So \(\int_{\{|h|<w\}}|h'|\le2wP\), and summing over \(k\) gives (1).

(2) By (4.81)–(4.82) of [T4], each \(f_{cq,\ell}\) is continuous and piecewise linear with breakpoints at \((\ell-1)2^{-cq}\), \((\ell-\frac12)2^{-cq}\), \(\ell2^{-cq}\), all in \(2^{-cr-1}\mathbb Z\) for \(q\le r\). So every \(f_k\), and every difference \(h\), is linear on each interval of the grid \(2^{-cr-1}\mathbb Z\cap[0,1]\): \(P\le2^{cr+1}\le2N^{1/100}\). Step 3 of lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound gives \(\psi^*_\alpha(y)\le2(\log N)^{1/\alpha}y\) for \(y\le N\), so \(\rho=2(\log N)^{1/\alpha_1}\le2\log N\) with \(Y=N\). By (4.88) and disjoint supports within a level, \(|F_k'|\le r/\sqrt r=\sqrt r\le\sqrt{\log N}\), so \(A_k|f_k'|\le\kappa_k\sqrt{\log N}\le\log N\le wN\) for large \(N\), since \(w=\delta\ge s/64\), \(s=L\sqrt{\log N/N}\). With \(w\le s/32\) and \(\sum A_k\le m\), (1) gives \(\sigma\le2\cdot2\log N\cdot\frac{L}{32}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}N}\cdot2N^{1/100}\cdot m^2\), which is the bound.

(3) Let \(H_k=\mu_kG_k\) with \(\Phi(H_k)=2\) (it exists as in the normalization of lem:aligned-superposition-bound) and write \(a_k=\mu_kA_k\) for its amplitude. Lower bound on \(a_k\). For the band function \(G_k^{(a)}\) of amplitude \(a\) over \(f_k\), the gradients are at most \(a\max(1,|f_k'|)/w\le N\) when \(a\le1\), so by the linear upper bound on \(\psi^*\) used in (2), \(\Phi(G_k^{(a)})\le2\log N\int(|\partial_1G_k^{(a)}|+|\partial_2G_k^{(a)}|)\le2a\log N(1+\int_0^1|f_k'|)\le2a\log N(1+\kappa_k)\le4a(\log N)^{3/2}\), using \(\int|F_k'|\le(\int F_k'^2)^{1/2}\le1\) by (4.83)–(4.84). So \(\Phi\le2\) at \(a_0=\frac12(\log N)^{-3/2}\). The map \(a\mapsto\Phi(G_k^{(a)})\) is convex with value \(0\) at \(0\), hence strictly increasing where it is positive; as it equals \(2\) at \(a_k\) and is at most \(2\) at \(a_0\), \(a_k\ge a_0\). Gradients of \(H_k\). Its vertical gradient is \(a_k/w\ge a_0\cdot32/s\), and its horizontal gradient, where nonzero, is \(a_k\kappa_k|F_k'|/w\ge a_0(\log N)^{-1/2}r^{-1/2}\cdot32/s\), since \(F_k'\) is a sum of at most \(r\) terms \(\pm1/\sqrt r\), one per level (disjoint supports within a level), hence at least \(1/\sqrt r\) in absolute value where nonzero; both exceed \(N^{1/3}\) for large \(N\). Applying the aligned superposition bound. The proof of its part (2) uses the gradient hypothesis only to apply (N) to \(y=|\partial_jH_k|\) when \(\varepsilon\le\lambda_k\le1\) (and, for \(\alpha_j=1\), to have \(\lambda_ky>1\), which holds since \(\lambda_ky\ge\varepsilon N^{1/3}\)), and it uses \(\Phi(\sum G_k)\le2\) only through \(\sum_k\Phi(G_k)\le2+\sigma\), from part (1) of that lemma. Both hold here with \(\theta=1/3\), for every amplitude \(A_k\in(0,1]\). It gives \(\ell(\sum G_k)\le(1+\sigma/2)(1+c_0\log\log N/\log N)\max_k\ell(H_k)^+\), and \(\sigma\to0\) by (2). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. \(m=1\): \(\sigma=0\), and (1) holds trivially. Two equal graphs: \(h=0\) has one piece of slope \(0\), contributing nothing, and indeed equal graphs have aligned gradients. Graphs that never come within \(w\): the bands are disjoint and \(\sigma=0\). A family oriented with mixed signs (some members \(-G_k\)) is outside the statement: then \(\partial_2\) takes both signs where bands overlap, \(E_2\) can carry the members' full vertical penalty (for instance where two graphs vanish on a common interval), and (1) fails. So do members reflected in the square (\(\partial_2>0\)) and transposed members (bands around \(x=g(y)\)), which the review's own symmetries (\(\kappa\to1/\kappa\) by exchanging the coordinates) and the class test's transposition for \(\beta_1<\beta_2\) make natural members of a superposition of its kind; they are outside this statement. \(\alpha_j=\infty\) is excluded as in the aligned superposition bound.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\), \(L\ge132\): aspects \(\kappa_k=2^k\), \(|k|\le\frac14\log_2\log N\), so \(m\le\frac12\log_2\log N+1\) and \(\kappa_k\in[(\log N)^{-1/4},(\log N)^{1/4}]\); \(F_k\) the Leighton–Shor function of Step 1 at each aspect (any choice of tents serves); amplitudes \(A_k=(\log N)^{-1/4}/(2m(1+\kappa_k))\). Then \(\Phi(\sum G_k)\le1\): pointwise, \(\psi^*_4(\sum_ka_k)\le2(\log N)^{1/4}\sum_ka_k\) for \(a_k\ge0\) with \(\sum a_k\le N\) (Step 3's bound), so \(\Phi(\sum_kG_k)\le2(\log N)^{1/4}\sum_k\int(|\partial_1G_k|+|\partial_2G_k|)\le2(\log N)^{1/4}\sum_kA_k(1+\int_0^1|f_k'|)\le\sum_k2(\log N)^{1/4}A_k(1+\kappa_k)=1\), using a band of width \(w\) and \(\int|F_k'|\le(\int F_k'^2)^{1/2}\le1\) by (4.83)–(4.84). Every hypothesis of (2) and (3) holds, with \(\sigma\le N^{-1/2+1/50}\).

What it gives. The bands of Leighton–Shor test functions at different aspects overlap with opposite horizontal slopes only on a set whose penalty is \(O(m^2(\log N)^{3/2}N^{-0.49})\): their graphs are piecewise linear on a grid of \(2N^{1/100}\) cells, and a difference of two such graphs stays within \(w\) of zero over a length \(2w/|s|\) per piece. So the review's superposition, with all members vertically scaled and positively oriented, is no better than \(1+o(1)\) times its best member rescaled to the full budget; a member of small amplitude counts through its rescaled form, whose gradients stay above \(N^{1/3}\), so small amplitudes need no separate bound. The single-member question remains: whether one scaled Leighton–Shor function off balance is worth more at full budget than at balance, which is the review's posed Operation and is bounded on record only from below.

Architecture test. Decided, in the class form, for the posed object with vertically scaled, positively oriented members: the second route review's superposition of positively oriented scaled Leighton–Shor test functions at \(m\le(\log N)^C\) aspects is worth at most \((1+o(1))\) times its best member at full budget, by (3), for members vertically scaled and oriented by their own (positive) discrepancy; the review's outcome "superpositions gain a bounded factor and cannot refute" occurred, sharpened to \(1+o(1)\), whatever the single-member decay. A refutation of conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy along this architecture therefore needs a single scaled Leighton–Shor function exceeding the bound, the single-function question. Not covered: reflected or transposed members, and the exact dual, which a refutation of the matching conjecture needs.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "band overlap penalty piecewise linear graphs crossing coarea" returned only claims of other notebooks; the nearest of this notebook are lem:aligned-superposition-bound and lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, which this entry applies. tools/claim_resemblance.py on the spec: nearest lem:aligned-superposition-bound (0.31, related in the spec as the lemma applied), all others below 0.16.

Follow-up. Developed: the item asked for \(\sigma\) and whether it puts the review's superposition under the aligned superposition bound; both are answered, \(\sigma=O(m^2(\log N)^{3/2}N^{-1/2+1/100})\) and yes for vertically scaled, positively oriented members, so no application of the item to an open statement remains; reflected or transposed members are outside the item, which asked about the review's superposition as built; the reviewer read this removal (reviewer's record). Not examined. The item names the graphs' crossings; the proof counts linear pieces instead, which bounds the crossings' contribution and needs no crossing count, so nothing it names is left. Owed attempt. The attempt named by the item's queued text was made, in the sharper form above.

New mathematics. lem:band-overlap-penalty: for band functions over piecewise linear graphs, the misaligned overlap penalty is at most \(2\rho wP(m-1)\sum A_k\); for scaled Leighton–Shor functions it is \(O(m^2(\log N)^{3/2}N^{-1/2+1/100})\), so by lem:aligned-superposition-bound a superposition of them at \(m\le(\log N)^C\) aspects is worth at most \(1+o(1)\) times its best member at full budget, for vertically scaled, positively oriented members.

Goal progress. Unchanged: no implication toward conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy or conj:ultimate-matching changed; one refutation architecture for the class form is closed for vertically scaled, positively oriented members. Bearing. On the curve's parameters, at every pair of finite exponents, combining vertically scaled, positively oriented Leighton–Shor test functions across aspects cannot beat the best single one by more than \(1+o(1)\) in the class, so a refutation of the class bound through such members needs one of them, rescaled to the full budget, beyond \((1-o(1))\frac L2\sqrt{N\log N}\) by itself. Not covered. One member's value off balance (the posed Operation), mixed orientations, reflected or transposed members, \(\alpha_j=\infty\), and the exact dual.

Review. The stand-in review did not run at the last checkpoint; nothing is owed.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Single member below balance: a level-by-level union bound over the family (4.122) (at level \(q\), \(2^{O(2^{cq})}\) tent choices, layers of measure about \(\kappa2^{-cq}/\sqrt r\), Bernstein) bounds the excess of every set under \(\kappa F\) by \(O(\sqrt{\kappa N}(\log N)^{3/4})\) uniformly, with no Leighton–Shor upper bound; with \(\psi^*(y)\ge cy(\log y)^\beta\) at \(y=N^{1/2\pm o(1)}\) it would give \(V(\kappa)\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa/\kappa^*}\,V(\kappa^*)\) for \(\kappa\le\kappa^*\); the cell smearing must be kept as cell sums, since errors of order \(AN\delta\) are as large as \(V(\kappa^*)\) on the curve. Above balance the penalty charges \(\int|f'|\) while the excess is governed by \(\int f'^2\), and sparse tent choices make \(\int|F'|\) small; the reviewer found no proof there. Record. tools/search-claims.py "union bound tent family excess Bernstein upper bound Leighton Shor value aspect below balance" returned lem:anisotropic-leighton-shor-bound, lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound and thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion, none an upper bound on one member's value.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the proof of lem:aligned-superposition-bound, Steps 1–4 of lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, (4.81)–(4.89) and (4.122) of [T4], and the second review's Decisive test. One fresh-context medium reviewer read the draft, spec, commit message and those excerpts, and failed the first draft: parts (1) and (2) held, but part (3) carried an additive error \(2mN^{3/4}\) for members of amplitude below \(N^{-1/4}\), far above the scale \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) being decided, so the decision did not follow; it also found reflected and transposed members missing from Not covered, convexity unused in (1), and the members' common \(c,r\) unstated. Part (3) was rewritten: the proof of the aligned superposition bound needs its gradient hypothesis only for the rescaled \(H_k\), whose amplitude is at least \(\frac12(\log N)^{-3/2}\), so there is no amplitude split and no additive error. The same reviewer's follow-up read the rewrite and passed it, with corrections made: the amplitude bound needs strict monotonicity (convexity with value \(0\) at \(0\)), \(N_0\) depends on \(L\), and the Bearing and the Architecture test carry the members' restriction. The follow-up also reviewed the framework commit that builds finisher-not-run-retrieval (record in this entry's results). Process audit. On the title it proposed "Leighton–Shor bands at several aspects overlap with o(1) penalty", which the title follows. Its first report found the commit message, spec and Bearing claiming a decision the additive error did not support, and "one computation decides it" in Chosen wrong; Follow-up Developed is justified once the additive error is gone.

Process assessment. A decision was drawn from an inequality without comparing its additive error with the scale decided, and an orientation claim ignored the review's own symmetries. Prevention. AGENTS.md now requires a decision sentence to restate its bound's hypotheses and error terms beside the instance and scale decided; the finisher check is queued as error-scale-line.

Meta. Lipschitz functions force order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) at every exponent pair

16 October 2026 · Working proof; a lower bound, all pairs simultaneously · Lipschitz baseline for the class. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Chosen. The head entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3 is developed through its sub-idea entry-2026-10-16-leighton-shor-class-test:s1. Its earlier developments: the Hopf–Lax reduction ("The reduction is proved, including the endpoints and the comparison with weights at least linear"), the Leighton–Shor class test ("The reduced statement is not refuted by its first test"), the dyadic flow bound ("The item now has a primal criterion and a calibration"), the dyadic flow obstruction ("The item reaches its spell boundary with this entry and rotates"), the adaptive circulation test ("gave a constant change of shape and no growing gap") and the stopped convex flow run ("its numerical branch is closed at reachable sizes"). The sub-idea bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy only as a constraint on its sharpness, not toward proving it; it comes before the open upper bound, the higher-risk direction, because it is short and settles what that upper bound must reach at the endpoints, which no statement on record did. It asks whether the class's lower bound of order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) holds at every pair, endpoints included, which the Leighton–Shor class test proved only at interior pairs of the curve and left unchecked against the elementary Lipschitz baseline.

Owed attempt. The item's latest follow-up named "a flow built level by level that, at each level, moves only the excess that would exceed a threshold". Not made in this entry: the sub-idea came first because it is a few-line lemma from two read theorems of [T4] that fixes what any proof of the class bound must reach. The next entry on the item makes the attempt, or drops it with a reason it finds.

Question. Does the Ajtai–Komlós–Tusnády lower bound for 1-Lipschitz functions survive the cell smearing of the reduction, so that \(D_N\gtrsim\sqrt{N\log N}\) at every pair?

General statement. lem:lipschitz-class-lower-bound, for every pair \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in[1,\infty]\) and every \(L\ge132\).

Lipschitz class lower bound

Setting. As in cor:exponential-matching-discrepancy-reduction: \(X_1,\dots,X_N\) i.i.d. uniform on \(\Omega=[0,1]^2\), \(Y\) evenly spread (rectangles \(R_j\) of area \(1/N\), \(Y_j\in R_j\subset B(Y_j,10/\sqrt N)\)), \(L\ge132\), \(s=L\sqrt{\log N/N}\), the grid of \(M=\lceil32/s\rceil^2\) cells of side \(\delta=1/\lceil32/s\rceil\), \(\mu_X=\sum_i\mathrm{Unif}(Q(X_i))\) and \(\mu_Y=\sum_j\mathrm{Unif}(Q(Y_j))\), \(Q(z)\) the cell of \(z\), and \(D_N=\sup\{\int G\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y):G\text{ Lipschitz},\ \int_\Omega\sum_j\psi^*_{\alpha_j}(|\partial_jG|)\le2\}\).

Statement. There is a universal constant \(L_0\) such that for every \(L\ge132\), with probability tending to one, \(D_N\ge\sqrt{N\log N}/(4L_0)\) for all pairs \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in[1,\infty]\) simultaneously. More generally the same holds, on the same event, for the supremum over every class of Lipschitz functions that contains \(\mathcal C^*\) (defined below), for instance the class of any separable penalty \(\int\sum_j\varphi_j(|\partial_jG|)\le2\) with \(\varphi_j\) nondecreasing and \(\varphi_j(1)\le1\).

Proof. The class contains the 1-Lipschitz functions. For \(\alpha\in[1,\infty)\), \(\psi^*_\alpha\) is nondecreasing and \(\psi^*_\alpha(1)=\sup_{t\ge0}(t-e^{t^\alpha}+1)\le\sup_{t\ge0}(t-t^\alpha)\le1\), since \(e^x-1\ge x\) and \(t-t^\alpha\le t\le1\) for \(t\le1\), \(\le0\) for \(t>1\); and \(\psi^*_\infty(y)=y\) for \(y\le N\). So every \(f\) in the class \(\mathcal C^*\) of [T4] (1-Lipschitz for the Euclidean distance, zero on \(\partial\Omega\)) has \(|\partial_jf|\le1\) almost everywhere and \(\int\sum_j\psi^*_{\alpha_j}(|\partial_jf|)\le2\), hence \(D_N\ge\sup_{f\in\mathcal C^*}\int f\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)\). Write \(\bar f_Q\) for the average of \(f\) over a cell \(Q\) and \(N_Q=\#\{i:X_i\in Q\}\). Since \(N\int f\,d\lambda=\sum_QN|Q|\bar f_Q\), \[\int f\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)=\underbrace{\sum_i\Big(f(X_i)-\int f\,d\lambda\Big)}_{A(f)}-\underbrace{\sum_i\big(f(X_i)-\bar f_{Q(X_i)}\big)}_{B(f)}-\underbrace{\sum_Q\big(\#\{j:Y_j\in Q\}-N|Q|\big)\bar f_Q}_{C(f)}.\]

The main term. Theorem 4.6.1 of [T4] gives \(\mathbb E\sup_{\mathcal C^*}A\ge\sqrt{N\log N}/L_0\). Every \(f\in\mathcal C^*\) has \(|f|\le1/2\), so changing one \(X_i\) changes \(\sup_{\mathcal C^*}A\) by at most \(1\), and McDiarmid's inequality gives \(P(\sup A\le\mathbb E\sup A-t)\le e^{-2t^2/N}\); with \(t=\sqrt{N\log N}/(2L_0)\) this is \(N^{-1/(2L_0^2)}\to0\). So \(\sup_{\mathcal C^*}A\ge\sqrt{N\log N}/(2L_0)\) with probability tending to one.

The \(Y\) side. \(N|Q|=\sum_jN|R_j\cap Q|\), so \(C(f)=\sum_j\sum_Qd_{jQ}\bar f_Q\) with \(d_{jQ}=1\{Y_j\in Q\}-N|R_j\cap Q|\), \(\sum_Qd_{jQ}=0\) and \(\sum_Q|d_{jQ}|\le2\). If \(R_j\) lies in the interior of one cell, that cell contains \(Y_j\) and \(d_{jQ}=0\) for all \(Q\); otherwise \(R_j\) meets or touches a grid line, and every \(Q\) with \(d_{jQ}\ne0\) meets \(B(Y_j,10/\sqrt N)\), so \(|\bar f_Q-f(Y_j)|\le10/\sqrt N+\sqrt2\delta\) and \(|\sum_Qd_{jQ}\bar f_Q|=|\sum_Qd_{jQ}(\bar f_Q-f(Y_j))|\le2(10/\sqrt N+\sqrt2\delta)\). The rectangles meeting a grid line lie in the \(20/\sqrt N\)-neighbourhood of the grid lines, of area at most \(2(1/\delta+1)\cdot40/\sqrt N\), and are disjoint of area \(1/N\), so there are at most \(80(1/\delta+1)\sqrt N\) of them. Hence \(\sup_f|C(f)|\le160(1/\delta+1)\sqrt N(10/\sqrt N+\sqrt2\delta)=1600(1/\delta+1)+160\sqrt2(1+\delta)\sqrt N\), which is \(O(\sqrt N)\) since \(\delta\le1\) and \(1/\delta=\lceil32/s\rceil\le32/s+1=O(\sqrt{N/\log N})\).

The \(X\) side. Given the cells of all points, the points in a cell \(Q\) are i.i.d. uniform on \(Q\). For each cell, \(\sup_g\sum_{i:X_i\in Q}(g(X_i)-\bar g_Q)\) over 1-Lipschitz \(g\) on \(Q\) is, after scaling \(Q\) to \(\Omega\), \(\delta\) times the same quantity \(W_{N_Q}\) for \(N_Q\) uniform points in \(\Omega\) and the class \(\mathcal C\) of 1-Lipschitz functions; by Theorem 4.5.3 of [T4], \(\mathbb EW_n\le L_1\sqrt{n\log n}\) for \(n\ge2\) (the extraction states no range; \(n\ge2\) is read as in Theorem 4.5.1, the bound being false at \(n=1\)), and \(W_1\le\sqrt2\), \(W_0=0\), so \(\mathbb EW_n\le L_2\sqrt{n\log(n+2)}\) for all \(n\ge0\). As \(\mathcal C\) is symmetric, \(\sup_{\mathcal C^*}|B|\le\sum_Q\delta W^{(Q)}\). Let \(E\) be the event that \(N_Q\le2N\delta^2\) for every cell; \(N\delta^2\ge Ns^2/4096=L^2\log N/4096\ge4\log N\), so the Chernoff bound \(P(N_Q\ge2N\delta^2)\le e^{-N\delta^2/3}\le N^{-4/3}\) and \(M\le N\) give \(P(E)\to1\). Given the cell counts, on \(E\), \[\mathbb E\Big[\sup|B|\,\Big|\,(N_Q)\Big]\le\delta L_2\sqrt{\ell_0}\sum_Q\sqrt{N_Q}\le\delta L_2\sqrt{\ell_0}\sqrt{MN}=L_2\sqrt{\ell_0N},\] with \(\ell_0=\log(2N\delta^2+2)\le\log(L^2\log N)\) for large \(N\), using Cauchy–Schwarz and \(\delta\sqrt M=1\). By Markov's inequality, \(P(\sup|B|>\sqrt{N\log N}/(8L_0),E)\le8L_0L_2\sqrt{\ell_0/\log N}\to0\).

Conclusion. With probability tending to one, taking \(f\in\mathcal C^*\) with \(A(f)\ge\sqrt{N\log N}/(2L_0)-1\), \(D_N\ge A(f)-\sup|B|-\sup|C|\ge\frac{\sqrt{N\log N}}{2L_0}-\frac{\sqrt{N\log N}}{8L_0}-O(\sqrt N)-1\ge\frac{\sqrt{N\log N}}{4L_0}\) for large \(N\) (depending on \(L\)). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. \(\alpha_1=\alpha_2=1\): \(\psi^*_1(y)=0\) for \(y\le1\), so the 1-Lipschitz functions cost nothing and the bound holds, as it must, the class being larger. \(\alpha_j=\infty\): \(\psi^*_\infty(1)=1\), the class still contains \(\mathcal C^*\), and the endpoints \((2,\infty)\), \((\infty,2)\), excluded from lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, are covered. \(N\) small: the statement is asymptotic. A configuration with \(X\) evenly spread instead of random: this proof's main term \(A\) is then \(O(\sqrt N)\) by (4.41) of [T4], so the proof gives nothing; whether \(D_N\) itself is small then is not shown, the class holding functions of large gradient.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters: the pair \((4,4)\), or the endpoint \((2,\infty)\), with \(L=132\); every hypothesis is a choice of parameters, and the conclusion is about the random \(X\).

What it gives. At every pair, the class's supremum is at least of order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) through the 1-Lipschitz functions alone, so the order in conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy cannot be lowered anywhere, endpoints included; the Leighton–Shor class lower bound adds nothing to the order on the curve (it reaches the same order at interior pairs) and matters only below the curve, where it exceeds it. This is what that lemma's own Statement left open ("this baseline was not checked, so (b) is not claimed as the lemma's specific contribution").

Architecture test. Decided. The second route review's test was decided in the class form, for vertically scaled, positively oriented members, by the band overlap entry (the outcome "superpositions gain a bounded factor" occurred, sharpened to \(1+o(1)\)); this entry adds that a single member cannot be pushed below order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) either, so any refutation through the class must come from one function's constant, not its order.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "Ajtai Komlos Tusnady lower bound Lipschitz class cell smearing penalized discrepancy endpoints" returned lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy and the dyadic flow theorems, none a Lipschitz lower bound for the class. tools/claim_resemblance.py on the spec: lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound (0.75), conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy (0.53) and cor:exponential-matching-discrepancy-reduction (0.37), all related in the spec; the first is the lemma whose baseline this one supplies.

Follow-up. Continuing. The item still bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy: this entry settled one of its sub-ideas; the parent's next attempt is the threshold flow named above, to be made or dropped in the next entry on the item.

  • Follow-up. Continuing: not attempted in this entry.

The threshold flow needs a layered cut bound the record lacks; dropped

16 October 2026 · No result; owed attempt made and dropped · Threshold flow. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Where we stand. Unchanged: The entry drops an owed attempt and proves nothing; no statement summarized in Where we stand changed.

Chosen. The head entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3, by default; its latest development, the Lipschitz class lower bound, left the threshold flow "to be made or dropped in the next entry on the item".

Owed attempt. The item's latest follow-ups named "a flow built level by level that, at each level, moves only the excess that would exceed a threshold, and a bound on its \(\psi_4\) cost through the Leighton–Shor upper bound at each threshold". Made, as an analysis of what it needs, and dropped, for the reason below.

Question. Can a flow whose size is cut into layers by thresholds \(T_1<T_2<\dots\) have its \(\psi_4\) cost bounded through the Leighton–Shor upper bound applied at each threshold, giving the primal form of conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\)?

General statement. The attempt probed conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\) through its primal form (lem:flow-matching-bound); it registers no claim.

What a threshold flow needs

Layered capacities. A flow moving at each point only excess above a threshold is, made precise, a flow \(\Phi\) with \(|\Phi_j|\le w_j\) for capacities \(w_j=\sum_kT_k1_{A_{j,k}}\), the layer \(A_{j,k}\) being where the \(k\)-th threshold is used; its cost in the flow bound is at most \(\lambda'\sum_j\int\psi_4(w_j/(\lambda's))\). In the convention of lem:flow-matching-bound, \(\int\nabla g\cdot\Phi=\int g\,d(\nu_X-\nu_Y)\) for every Lipschitz \(g\), \(\nu_X,\nu_Y\) the smeared measures; so any such flow gives \[\Big|\int g\,d(\nu_X-\nu_Y)\Big|\le\int_\Omega(w_1|\partial_1g|+w_2|\partial_2g|)\,dx\quad\text{for every Lipschitz }g,\] and, by the coarea formula, the cut inequality \(|(\nu_X-\nu_Y)(U)|\le\int_{\partial U\cap\operatorname{int}\Omega}(w_1|n_1|+w_2|n_2|)\,d\mathcal H^1\) for almost every level set \(U\) of \(g\). A threshold flow therefore exists only if discrepancies are bounded by boundary capacities in this sense. (The converse is a continuous max-flow min-cut theorem, Strang (1983) for isotropic capacities and, for box constraints, Nozawa (1990), both recalled by the reviewer and not read, the latter needing regular capacities; neither is used.)

What the Leighton–Shor bound supplies. The Leighton–Shor upper bound (Theorem 4.7.1 of [T4], as used in lem:anisotropic-leighton-shor-bound) gives, with high probability, a matching with every displacement at most \(d=K(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\). Its smeared segment flow has density about \(Nd\) provided the \(X\)-counts in strips of width \(d\) are close to their means, a uniform local count that lem:anisotropic-leighton-shor-bound does not state; granting it, the flow is one at the single constant capacity \(w\approx KN^{1/2}(\log N)^{3/4}\), and the corresponding heuristic is that every set's discrepancy is at most about \(Nd\) times its (anisotropic) perimeter, up to an additive \(d^2\) term. At \((4,4)\) with \(\lambda'=N/4\), \(w/(\lambda's)=(4K/L)(\log N)^{1/4}\), and a flow at that capacity costs up to \(2\cdot\frac N4\big(N^{(4K/L)^4}-1\big)\), unbounded against \(N\) for every fixed \(L\): one threshold is the maximum-displacement bound, which the maximum-displacement threshold already gives and which is far from the exponential sum.

What layering would need. For a layered capacity of bounded cost the high thresholds \(T_k\) must sit on sets \(A_k\) of measure at most about \(e^{-(T_k/\lambda's)^4}\) (Markov's inequality against the cost budget), and the cut condition then requires the boundary of every set \(U\) with \(|(\mu_X-\mu_Y)(U)|>T_{k-1}\operatorname{Per}(U)\) to run, in capacity, mostly inside \(A_k\cup A_{k+1}\cup\cdots\). The Leighton–Shor bound is a statement about each set at the top threshold; it says nothing about where the boundaries of all sets above an intermediate threshold lie, nor how much measure their union takes. A bound of that kind, uniform over all sets and all thresholds, is a multiscale discrepancy statement of the same reach as conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy itself; this is an assessment, not a proved equivalence. So the attempt as named, Leighton–Shor at each threshold, has no step that bounds the intermediate layers.

Degenerate cases. One layer: the constant capacity above. Thresholds at the Ajtai–Komlós–Tusnády scale only (\(T\approx\lambda's\)): the cut condition then asks every set's discrepancy to be at most \(\lambda's\) times its perimeter, which fails for the Leighton–Shor sets: by the Leighton–Shor lower bound (Theorem 4.8.1 of [T4], in the scaled form of lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, Step 1) some set of perimeter \(O(1)\) has discrepancy about \(\sqrt N(\log N)^{3/4}\), larger by \((\log N)^{1/4}\). This rules out the constant capacity at that scale only, not a layered one.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py for "capacity max-flow min-cut layered threshold flow cut condition perimeter discrepancy" returned only claims of other notebooks (Tseitin flows, cut transport), none on flows of point sets. The dyadic flow obstruction lists among its reviewer's unverified suggestions: "Truncate-and-correct is a reparametrization of the open statement"; this analysis is consistent with it.

Follow-up. Continuing. The owed attempt is dropped: the threshold flow needs a uniform bound on where the boundaries of all sets above each intermediate threshold lie, which the Leighton–Shor bound does not give and which this entry assesses, without proof, to be of the reach of the open statement. The item still bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy through its open sub-ideas; its next attempt is the sub-idea entry-2026-10-16-hopf-lax-reduction:s1, the converse of the Hopf–Lax bound, first in its tube-density form: state precisely the smearing under which a good matching's segment measure must have density at most \(KN\), and test it on the matching of the dyadic flow at \((2,2)\), where the class bound is known in order. Record search. The search "segment flow density good matching Orlicz cost Jensen tube interpolated density" of the second review returned only lem:flow-matching-bound; the converse is unregistered.

Sub-idea follow-up

  • Follow-up. Continuing: named as the item's next attempt.
  • Follow-up. Continuing: not attempted in this entry.
  • Follow-up. Continuing: not attempted in this entry; it is the converse's tube-density form and goes with the next attempt.

New mathematics. None beyond an analysis: a threshold flow is a flow under a layered capacity, which exists only if every set's discrepancy is at most its boundary's capacity; the Leighton–Shor bound supplies one constant capacity, costing \(\frac N2(N^{(4K/L)^4}-1)\) at \((4,4)\), and nothing about the intermediate layers.

Tube density converse: class bound from good matchings of bounded density

16 October 2026 · Working proof; a sufficient condition, not an equivalence · Converse of the Hopf–Lax bound. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Chosen. The head entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3, through its sub-idea entry-2026-10-16-hopf-lax-reduction:s1, as its latest development, the threshold flow dropped, named: "the converse of the Hopf–Lax bound, first in its tube-density form". It bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy: a converse would make the class bound equivalent to the conjecture, so that the route asks no more than the goal (the second route review's Extra requirements).

Owed attempt. The latest follow-up named: "state precisely the smearing under which a good matching's segment measure must have density at most \(KN\), and test it on the matching of the dyadic flow at \((2,2)\)". Made in its first part (the statement below, with the smearing that works: translation of whole cells). The \((2,2)\) control waits because the statement came first, which fixes what the control must measure, and because the matching is not explicit on record (thm:dyadic-flow-square-exponential obtains it through the flow bound and duality), so the control needs a matching built first; the plan, carried into the next attempt, is to build one by composing per-scale dyadic matchings and test the reviewer's location-dependent condition on it, which tolerates density peaks where displacements are short.

Question. Does a matching that satisfies the conjecture's condition bound the penalized discrepancy of the class, and under what condition on the matching?

General statement. lem:tube-density-converse, for all point sets, all grids, all bijections and all convex separable costs.

Tube density converse

Setting. \(\Omega=[0,1]^2\) cut into a grid of square cells of side \(\delta\); \(N\)-point sets \(X,Y\subset\Omega\), \(\mu_X=\sum_i\mathrm{Unif}(Q(X_i))\), \(\mu_Y=\sum_j\mathrm{Unif}(Q(Y_j))\), \(Q(z)\) the cell of \(z\); a bijection \(\pi\), \(\Delta_i=Y_{\pi(i)}-X_i\), and \(D_i=c(Q(Y_{\pi(i)}))-c(Q(X_i))\) the difference of the cells' centres, so \(|D_i^j-\Delta_i^j|\le\delta\) and \(Q(Y_{\pi(i)})=Q(X_i)+D_i\). The tube density of \(\pi\) is \[\rho_\pi(x)=\delta^{-2}\sum_i\operatorname{Leb}\{t\in[0,1]:x-tD_i\in Q(X_i)\},\] the density of the measure swept by the cells \(Q(X_i)+tD_i\), \(t\in[0,1]\), each of mass one; \(\int_\Omega\rho_\pi=N\), since every swept cell lies in the convex \(\Omega\). For convex \(\varphi_j:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty]\) with \(\varphi_j(0)=0\), \(\varphi_j^*\) is the convex conjugate.

Statement. (1) For every Lipschitz \(G\) and every \(\lambda>0\), \[\int G\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)\le\lambda\sum_{j=1,2}\Big[\sum_i\varphi_j\Big(\frac{|D_i^j|}\lambda\Big)+\int_\Omega\varphi_j^*(|\partial_jG|)\,\rho_\pi\,dx\Big].\] (2) Let \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in[1,\infty]\), \(\varphi_j=\psi_{\alpha_j}\) (\(\psi_\alpha(u)=e^{u^\alpha}-1\) for finite \(\alpha\), \(\psi_\infty(u)=N(u-1)_+\)), \(s>0\) and \(\delta\le s\). If \(\sum_i\psi_{\alpha_j}(|\Delta_i^j|/s)\le N\) for \(j=1,2\) (the conjecture's condition at scale \(s\) for finite \(\alpha_j\); for \(\alpha_j=\infty\) it holds after doubling \(s\) when \(\max_i|\Delta_i^j|\le2s\), the reduction's endpoint form), and \(\rho_\pi\le KN\) on \(\Omega\), then every Lipschitz \(G\) with \(\int\sum_j\psi^*_{\alpha_j}(|\partial_jG|)\le2\) has \[\int G\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)\le2(e+2K)\,sN.\]

Proof. (1) Since \(Q(Y_{\pi(i)})=Q(X_i)+D_i\) and both smearings are uniform, \(\int G\,d\mu_Y=\sum_i\operatorname{avg}_{z\in Q(X_i)}G(z+D_i)\), so \[\int G\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)=\sum_i\operatorname{avg}_{z\in Q(X_i)}\big(G(z)-G(z+D_i)\big)=-\sum_i\operatorname{avg}_{z}\int_0^1\nabla G(z+tD_i)\cdot D_i\,dt,\] valid for almost every \(z\) since \(G\) is Lipschitz (Rademacher and Fubini). By the Fenchel–Young inequality, coordinatewise, \(|\partial_jG|\,|D_i^j|=\lambda\cdot\frac{|D_i^j|}\lambda\cdot|\partial_jG|\le\lambda\big(\varphi_j(|D_i^j|/\lambda)+\varphi_j^*(|\partial_jG|)\big)\). Summing over \(i\) and \(j\), the first terms give \(\lambda\sum_j\sum_i\varphi_j(|D_i^j|/\lambda)\), and the second give \(\lambda\sum_j\sum_i\operatorname{avg}_z\int_0^1\varphi_j^*(|\partial_jG(z+tD_i)|)dt=\lambda\sum_j\int\varphi_j^*(|\partial_jG|)\rho_\pi\,dx\), because \(z+tD_i\) is uniform on \(Q(X_i)+tD_i\), of density \(\delta^{-2}\) there. \(\int\rho_\pi=N\), one for each swept cell.

(2) Take \(\lambda=2s\). Each \(\psi_\alpha\), \(\alpha\ge1\), is convex with \(\psi(0)=0\) and \(\psi\ge0\), hence nondecreasing; by convexity and \(|D_i^j|\le|\Delta_i^j|+\delta\), \(\psi(|D_i^j|/(2s))\le\frac12\psi(|\Delta_i^j|/s)+\frac12\psi(\delta/s)\le\frac12\psi(|\Delta_i^j|/s)+\frac12\psi(1)\), and \(\psi_\alpha(1)\le e-1\) (with \(\psi_\infty(1)=0\)); so \(\sum_j\sum_i\psi_{\alpha_j}(|D_i^j|/(2s))\le2\big(\frac N2+\frac{(e-1)N}2\big)=eN\). The second term is at most \(\|\rho_\pi\|_\infty\int\sum_j\psi^*_{\alpha_j}(|\partial_jG|)\le2KN\). So (1) gives \(2s(eN+2KN)\). \(\square\)

What it gives. A chain, not an equivalence: the conjecture at a pair together with a good matching of tube density at most \(KN\) on a grid of side at most \(s\) gives the class bound \(D_N\le2(e+2K)sN\) on that grid, and the class bound gives the conjecture by the reduction (cor:exponential-matching-discrepancy-reduction). The forward reduction produces a matching with no control of its tube density, so the two statements are not shown equivalent on any class of matchings. Bounded tube density is shown sufficient for the converse, not necessary; the reviewer's location-dependent version below weakens it. The registered conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy fixes a normalization (the factor \(\frac12\) and the grid \(s/32\) of the same \(L\)); matching it was not attempted here (the reviewer sketches below that it can be met when \(K<9-e\)). So the second route review's Extra requirements are bounded above: the route asks more than the goal at most by a sufficient density condition of this kind.

Degenerate cases. \(X=Y\), \(\pi\) the identity: \(D_i=0\), \(\rho_\pi(x)=\delta^{-2}\#\{i:X_i\in Q(x)\}\) and the bound is \(\lambda\sum_j\int\varphi_j^*(|\partial_jG|)\rho_\pi\), while the left side is \(0\). One point (\(N=1\)): \(\rho_\pi\) is the swept density of one cell, at most \(\delta^{-2}\), so \(K\) is then \(\delta^{-2}\), not a constant; the hypothesis \(\rho_\pi\le KN\) is meaningful only when every cell holds about its share of points, \(N\delta^2\gtrsim1\). \(K<1\) is impossible when the cells cover \(\Omega\) and \(\int\rho_\pi=N\).

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\): \(X=Y\) evenly spread (rectangles of area \(1/N\) inside balls of radius \(10/\sqrt N\)), \(\pi\) the identity, \(s=L\sqrt{\log N/N}\) with \(L\ge132\), \(\delta=1/\lceil32/s\rceil\): the condition holds (\(\Delta_i=0\)), and each cell holds \(N\delta^2+O(\delta\sqrt N)\) points, so \(\rho_\pi\le2N\) for large \(N\), \(K=2\). Every hypothesis of (2) holds, though the conclusion is trivial there; a witness with random \(X\) needs a good matching of bounded tube density, the open question.

Architecture test. Decided. The second route review's test was decided in the class form, for vertically scaled, positively oriented members, by the band overlap entry; this entry gives an upper bound on the answer to the review's other question, kept queued in its Decision, how much more than the goal the route asks: at most a sufficient tube-density condition on one good matching.

Follow-up. Continuing. The item still bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy: lem:tube-density-converse makes a bounded-density good matching sufficient for the class bound. (A crossing count against the Leighton–Shor sets, named in the first draft, is met for every \(L\ge132\) and \(K\ge1\), as the reviewer showed, so it decides nothing and was dropped.) Record search. tools/search-claims.py "converse Hopf-Lax tube density translation smearing Fenchel Young matching bounds discrepancy" returned only the forward bounds; no per-scale dyadic matching is registered beyond thm:dyadic-flow-square-exponential.

Sub-idea follow-up

  • Follow-up. Continuing: the converse is proved for matchings of bounded tube density (lem:tube-density-converse); whether good matchings can have it is open.
  • Follow-up. Continuing: not attempted in this entry; lem:tube-density-converse would answer it for any good matching of bounded tube density below the curve.
  • Follow-up. Continuing: its question, the tube density of good matchings, now bounds the converse's missing step (a bounded density suffices; it is not shown necessary); this entry fixes the smearing (translation of whole cells) under which it is asked.

New mathematics. lem:tube-density-converse: transporting each cell's uniform mass to its partner cell by translation and applying the Fenchel–Young inequality along the swept cells bounds the smeared discrepancy of every Lipschitz function by the matching's cost plus the class penalty weighted by the tube density; so a good matching of tube density at most \(KN\) gives the class bound with constant \(2(e+2K)\).

A matching's segment flux is a flow of Jensen-bounded cost

16 October 2026 · Working proof; bounded tube density assumed · Flow form of the converse. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Where we stand. Unchanged: The entry's result is a flow form of the tube converse already summarized; Where we stand needs no new sentence.

Chosen. The head entry-2026-10-16-monograph-statements:s3, through its sub-idea entry-2026-10-16-dyadic-flow-bound:s1, as its latest development, the tube density converse, named. It bears on conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy through the question that entry left: what a good matching of bounded tube density gives.

Owed attempt. The latest follow-up named "the location-dependent form below (checked in this entry), tested at \((2,2)\) on a matching built by composing per-scale dyadic matchings". Dropped, with its reason: the lemma below shows that such a test measures whether the composed matching's segment flux is a flow of bounded cost, and the composed matching is not a good matching at \((2,2)\) on record (the greedy dyadic matching of Exercise 4.5.2 of [T4] matches leftovers of coarse squares across them, and its square-exponential sum was never bounded), so the test would rest on an unproved precondition; a better-posed test, on optimal matchings through a quantitative swap inequality, replaces it in the Follow-up.

Question. What flow does a good matching define, and when does that flow have bounded cost in the flow bound?

General statement. lem:segment-flow-cost, for all point sets, grids, bijections and convex costs vanishing at \(0\).

Segment flow cost

Setting. As in lem:tube-density-converse: cells of side \(\delta\), cell-uniform smearings \(\mu_X,\mu_Y\), a bijection \(\pi\), centre differences \(D_i\), and \(\rho_i(x)=\delta^{-2}\operatorname{Leb}\{t\in[0,1]:x-tD_i\in Q(X_i)\}\), \(\rho_\pi=\sum_i\rho_i\). The segment flux of \(\pi\) is \(\Phi_\pi=\sum_iD_i\rho_i\).

Statement. (1) \(\Phi_\pi\) is bounded and, for every Lipschitz \(g\), \(\int\nabla g\cdot\Phi_\pi\,dx=\int g\,d(\mu_Y-\mu_X)\): it is a flow from \(\mu_X\) to \(\mu_Y\), and \(-\Phi_\pi\) satisfies the convention \(\int\nabla g\cdot\Phi=\int g\,d(\mu_X-\mu_Y)\) of lem:flow-matching-bound. (2) For convex \(\psi_j:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)\) with \(\psi_j(0)=0\), every \(\lambda>0\) and every \(M>0\) with \(\rho_\pi\le M\) on \(\Omega\), \[\sum_j\int_\Omega M\,\psi_j\Big(\frac{|\Phi_{\pi,j}|}{\lambda M}\Big)dx\le\sum_j\sum_i\psi_j\Big(\frac{|D_i^j|}\lambda\Big).\] In particular, if the right side is at most \(2N\) and \(\lambda'>0\) is the density level of lem:flow-matching-bound, then the cost \(C_\mu(z)=\sum_j\psi_j(|z_j|/\mu)\) at scale \(\mu=\lambda M/\lambda'\), which is symmetric in \(z\), gives \(\lambda'\int C_\mu(-\Phi_\pi/\lambda')\,dx\le\frac{\lambda'}M\cdot2N\).

Proof. (1) The swept cells \(Q(X_i)+tD_i\), \(t\in[0,1]\), lie in \(\Omega\), which is convex and contains \(Q(X_i)\) and \(Q(X_i)+D_i\); so \(\int\rho_i=1\). Each \(\rho_i\le\delta^{-2}\), so \(|\Phi_\pi|\le\delta^{-2}\sum_i|D_i|\). For \(z\in Q(X_i)\), \(z+tD_i\) is uniform on \(Q(X_i)+tD_i\), so \(\int\nabla g\cdot D_i\rho_i\,dx=\operatorname{avg}_{z}\int_0^1\nabla g(z+tD_i)\cdot D_i\,dt=\operatorname{avg}_{z\in Q(X_i)}\big(g(z+D_i)-g(z)\big)\) (for almost every \(z\), \(g\) being Lipschitz), and summing over \(i\) gives \(\int g\,d\mu_Y-\int g\,d\mu_X\), since \(Q(X_i)+D_i=Q(Y_{\pi(i)})\).

(2) Jensen. At \(x\) with \(\rho_\pi(x)>0\), \(|\Phi_{\pi,j}(x)|\le\sum_i\rho_i(x)|D_i^j|\), and with the weights \(\rho_i(x)/\rho_\pi(x)\), monotonicity (a convex \(\psi\ge0\) with \(\psi(0)=0\) is nondecreasing) and convexity give \(\rho_\pi\psi_j(|\Phi_{\pi,j}|/(\lambda\rho_\pi))\le\sum_i\rho_i\psi_j(|D_i^j|/\lambda)\). Perspective. For \(a\ge0\), \(r\mapsto r\psi(a/r)\) is nonincreasing on \((0,\infty)\): for \(0<r_1<r_2\), \(a/r_2=\frac{r_1}{r_2}\cdot\frac a{r_1}+(1-\frac{r_1}{r_2})\cdot0\), so convexity and \(\psi(0)=0\) give \(\psi(a/r_2)\le\frac{r_1}{r_2}\psi(a/r_1)\). Since \(\rho_\pi\le M\), \(M\psi_j(|\Phi_{\pi,j}|/(\lambda M))\le\rho_\pi\psi_j(|\Phi_{\pi,j}|/(\lambda\rho_\pi))\); where \(\rho_\pi=0\), \(\Phi_\pi=0\) and both sides vanish. Integrating and using \(\int\rho_i=1\) gives (2). For the last sentence, \(\lambda'\int C_\mu(-\Phi_\pi/\lambda')=\lambda'\sum_j\int\psi_j(|\Phi_{\pi,j}|/(\lambda M))=\frac{\lambda'}M\cdot M\sum_j\int\psi_j(|\Phi_{\pi,j}|/(\lambda M))\le\frac{\lambda'}M\cdot2N\). \(\square\)

What it gives. Three statements at a pair, each up to constants, cost scales and the choice of grid: (F) some flow between the smeared measures has bounded cost in the flow bound; (C) the class bound; (M) the conjecture. The record has F ⇒ M (lem:flow-matching-bound), C ⇒ M (cor:exponential-matching-discrepancy-reduction) and F ⇒ C by the Fenchel–Young inequality applied pointwise to \(\nabla G\cdot\Phi\) (the argument of lem:tube-density-converse (1) with the weight \(\rho\) replaced by the constant density level). This entry adds: a good matching of tube density \(O(N)\) gives F, through its own segment flux, at cost scale \(\mu=\lambda M/\lambda'\), with costs in centre differences \(D_i\), which differ from \(X_i-Y_{\pi(i)}\) by at most \(\delta\) per coordinate as in lem:tube-density-converse. So a bounded-density good matching suffices for the converse; whether M ⇒ F and M ⇒ C are the same question would need C ⇒ F, a convex duality the record does not have. The dyadic flow obstruction shows that the dyadic flow does not have bounded cost above exponent \(2\); it says nothing about the segment flux of a good matching, which carries the matching's own displacements.

Degenerate cases. \(X=Y\), \(\pi\) the identity: \(\Phi_\pi=0\) and both sides of (2) vanish. One pair: \(\Phi_\pi=D_1\rho_1\) and (2) is Jensen for one term, tight when \(\rho_1\) is constant on its support and \(M\) equals it. \(M\) below \(\max\rho_\pi\): excluded, since the perspective step needs \(\rho_\pi\le M\). \(\alpha=\infty\): \(\psi_\infty(u)=N(u-1)_+\) is convex, finite and vanishes at \(0\), so it is covered.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\): \(X=Y\) evenly spread, \(\pi\) the identity, \(\lambda=s=L\sqrt{\log N/N}\), \(M=2N\) (each cell holds \(N\delta^2+O(\delta\sqrt N)\) points). With a nontrivial pair, \(Y\) the same set with two points exchanged that lie within \(s\) of each other in each coordinate (so the cost stays at most \(N\) per coordinate), \(\rho_\pi\le2N+2\delta^{-2}\), which is at most \(3N\) when \(N\delta^2\ge2\), as it is for \(L\ge132\) (\(N\delta^2\approx L^2\log N/1024\)).

Architecture test. Decided. The second route review's test was decided in the class form, for vertically scaled, positively oriented members, by the band overlap entry; this entry gives a sufficient condition for the review's other question, how much more than the goal the route asks: a good matching whose segment flux is a flow of bounded cost, which a bounded tube density guarantees.

Next attempt, after the route review now due: whether an optimal matching for the cost itself has tube density \(O(N)\), through a quantitative swap inequality (the reviewer's suggestion below, checked here for the quadratic cost): first the quadratic cost, where the time-\(t\) points of an optimal matching stay at least \(\max(1-t,t)\) times as far apart as the corresponding \(X\) or \(Y\) points, then \(\psi_\alpha\) with a bound on the ratio of second derivatives over displacements of at most about \(s\sqrt{\log N}\), where it can fail for pairs with very unequal displacements. Record search. tools/search-claims.py "displacement interpolation injectivity optimal matching intermediate points distinct convex cost" returned lem:hopf-lax-matching-bound and lem:flow-matching-bound only.

Sub-idea follow-up

  • Follow-up. Continuing: the converse now reads as F from M; not decided.
  • Follow-up. Continuing: not attempted in this entry.
  • Follow-up. Continuing: its tube flow is the segment flux of lem:segment-flow-cost, whose cost is bounded by the matching's when its tube density is \(O(N)\); whether that density can be bounded is open.

New mathematics. lem:segment-flow-cost: a matching's segment flux is a flow between the smeared measures, and, when the matching's tube density is at most \(M\), its flow cost at level \(M\) is at most the matching's cost (Jensen pointwise and the monotonicity of the perspective \(r\mapsto r\psi(a/r)\)).

Goal progress. Unchanged: no implication toward conj:ultimate-matching changed; the route's flow, class and conjecture statements are linked by F ⇒ M, F ⇒ C, C ⇒ M and, for good matchings of bounded tube density, by this entry's step to F. Bearing. On the curve's parameters, at every pair with \(\alpha_j\ge1\), a good matching of tube density at most \(KN\) has a segment flux of bounded flow cost at scale \(\mu=\lambda KN/\lambda'\); for the class bound this adds no new implication beyond lem:tube-density-converse (2), and what it adds is the flow form. Not covered. Good matchings without bounded tube density, the converse C ⇒ F, and the registered normalization.

Review. The stand-in review did not run at the last checkpoint; nothing is owed.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Quantitative swap inequality: for an optimal matching under the quadratic cost, with \(a=X_1-X_2\) and \(b\) the difference of the displacements, optimality against the swap gives \(\langle a,a+b\rangle\ge0\), hence \(|a+tb|^2\ge(1-t)^2|a|^2\); so at times \(t\le\frac12\) the moving points stay at least half as far apart as the \(X\) points, and symmetrically from the \(Y\) side for \(t\ge\frac12\), which would bound the tube density by a constant times the maximal cell density of \(X\) and \(Y\), \(O(N)\) with high probability, up to an \(O(\delta)\) error from cell centres; for \(\psi_\alpha\) the argument holds only within the ratio of second derivatives, unbounded near \(0\) for \(1<\alpha<2\) and at large displacements. Record. tools/search-claims.py "swap inequality quadratic cost optimal matching time t points separation Beckmann min-cost flow duality grid" returned lem:hopf-lax-matching-bound and lem:flow-matching-bound only.
  • C ⇒ F by finite-dimensional convex duality on the grid (min-cost flow, Beckmann's problem), which with F ⇒ C would make the flow and class forms equivalent up to constants. Record. The same search; nothing registered.

Suggestion checked. The swap inequality was checked here for the quadratic cost: optimality against exchanging two partners gives \(|D_1|^2+|D_2|^2\le|D_2-a|^2+|D_1+a|^2\), that is \(\langle a,a+b\rangle\ge0\) with \(b=D_1-D_2\); and \(a+tb=(1-t)a+t(a+b)\), whose squared norm is \((1-t)^2|a|^2+t^2|a+b|^2+2t(1-t)\langle a,a+b\rangle\ge(1-t)^2|a|^2\). The step from point separation to a bound on the tube density was not checked.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: lem:tube-density-converse and lem:flow-matching-bound with their conventions. One fresh-context medium reviewer read the draft, spec, commit message and those statements, found the Jensen step, the perspective proof, the vanishing case and the arithmetic correct, and passed it with corrections, all made: the sign (the flow bound takes \(-\Phi_\pi\), the cost being symmetric); the flow bound's density level \(\lambda'\) is fixed, so the scale goes into the cost, \(\mu=\lambda M/\lambda'\), not \(\lambda'=KN\); the convexity of \(\Omega\) for the swept cells; What it gives no longer calls the converse "one question", which would need C ⇒ F, cites the Fenchel–Young step for F ⇒ C, and notes centre differences; the Bearing says it adds only the flow form beyond lem:tube-density-converse; an unproved remark in Degenerate cases removed; the Nonvacuity pair placed within \(s\); the owed \((2,2)\) test dropped with its reason; the qualitative injectivity attempt, which could only fail, replaced by the quantitative swap inequality. Process audit. It proposed the title "A matching's segment flux is a flow of Jensen-bounded cost", which the title follows; strength count 4 (the commit message's "converse question becomes", "one question in its cleanest form", the Bearing's \(\lambda'=KN\), the Degenerate cases' "indeed"), all corrected; it found the owed attempt deferred on a reason that called for running it, and a next step resting on an unread source.

Process assessment. The flow bound's density level was treated as a free parameter, and an equivalence was again claimed from one direction. Prevention. The queued build equivalence-word-check covers "one question"; the parameter slip falls under the reviewer definition's audit of results cited for what they give without their hypotheses (.claude/agents/medium-reviewer.md), which caught it.

Meta. Both slips came from reusing a lemma's form with my own parameter in place of the lemma's fixed one; the change is to copy a cited lemma's quantifiers (which parameters it fixes, which it leaves free) before applying it, which the same reviewer audit item now asks for.
