Log-log deviation term for the mesh and two-mesh discrepancy lemmas

16 October 2026 · Working proof; cascade not decided · Cascade over levels. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Where we stand. Unchanged: The entry refines a tool lemma and leaves the test undecided; the goal's state in Where we stand is unchanged.

Question. The two-mesh partition entry's reviewer sketched a cascade of its staircase step over geometrically spaced meshes, charging each edge within a constant of its own level's factor plus \(K\sqrt{\log N}\), \(K\) the number of levels, a loss at the target's order unless the per-level deviation is \(\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\). The missing implication is conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\), through the third route review's two-level test; this entry proves the smaller deviation and attempts the cascade.

General statement. lem:loglog-mesh-deviation, for every mesh up to the Leighton–Shor scale, every curve on it, every cell function and every graded partition.

A smaller deviation term

Statement. With probability tending to one and a universal \(L\): in lem:mesh-function-discrepancy (a) the factor becomes \((\log_2(2^{j+3}\ell(C)))^{3/4}+\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\); in its (b), and in lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy, the term \(\sqrt{\log N}\) beside \(j^{3/4}\) (beside \(j_1^{3/4}\), \(j_2^{3/4}\)) becomes \(\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\) (\(\sqrt{j_1+\log\log N}\), \(\sqrt{j_2+\log\log N}\)). For (b) and the two-mesh lemma, since \(\sqrt j\le j^{3/4}\), the factors are at most \(2j^{3/4}+\sqrt{\log\log N}\); for (a) that simplification would give up the short-curve factor. The deviation is \(\sqrt j\) rather than \(\sqrt m\) because of the union over the vertices \(\tau\). The failure probability is now of order \((\log N)^{-cL^2}\), not the \(LN^{-2}\log N\) of the earlier form; both events can be intersected, so the earlier statements and these hold together with probability tending to one.

Proof. Only the deviation step of Step 1 of lem:mesh-function-discrepancy changes. For a triple \((j,\tau,k)\), take \(u=L2^k\sqrt N\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\). For \(k\ge0\), the bounded-differences inequality (4.107) gives failure probability at most \(2\exp(-L^22^{2k}(j+\log\log N)/2)\le2e^{-L^2j/2}(\log N)^{-L^2/2}\). For \(k<0\): conditionally on \(I\) with \(\operatorname{card}I\le L'2^{2k}N\), the same inequality with \(\operatorname{card}I\) points gives at most \(2\exp(-L^2(j+\log\log N)/(2L'))\); the outside term \(|\operatorname{card}I-N\lambda(V)|\) exceeds \(u\) with probability at most \(2\exp(-u^2/(4\cdot2^{2k+2}N))=2\exp(-L^2(j+\log\log N)/16)\) by Bernstein's inequality, since its variance is at most \(2^{2k+2}N\) and \(u\) is below that variance bound for \(N\) large depending on \(L\) (\(2^k\sqrt N\ge4(\log N)^{3/4}\) while \(\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\le\sqrt{2\log N}\)); and \(\operatorname{card}I>L'2^{2k}N\) has probability at most \(e^{-16(\log N)^{3/2}}\) as before. The expectation bound \(L2^k\sqrt N(k+j+1)^{3/4}\) is unchanged. Summing over the triples, with at most \((2^j+1)^2(2j+5)\le4^{j+2}j\) of them for each \(j\), the failure probability is at most \(\sum_{j\ge1}4^{j+2}j\cdot6e^{-cL^2j}(\log N)^{-cL^2}+LN\log N\,e^{-16(\log N)^{3/2}}\), \(c=\min(\frac12,\frac1{2L'},\frac1{16})\), universal since \(L'\) is the fixed Chernoff constant, which tends to \(0\) once \(cL^2>\log4+1\). Steps 2 and 3 of that lemma, and Steps 1 to 3 of lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy, use only the curve bound, so they carry the new term. \(\square\)

Hypotheses, each with its step. As in lem:mesh-function-discrepancy: \(X_i\) i.i.d. uniform for the expectation and the deviations, and \(j\le\ell_1\) for \(2^j\le\sqrt N\) and the count in small squares. The new step uses in addition that \(u\) is below the Bernstein variance, which holds because \(j\le\ell_1\) gives \(2^k\sqrt N\ge4(\log N)^{3/4}\) for \(k\ge2-j\). The conclusion holds simultaneously for all meshes, curves, cell functions, graded partitions and constants.

Degenerate cases. \(j\) of order \(\log N\): \(\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\) is of order \(\sqrt{\log N}\), the old term. \(j\) bounded: the term is \(\sqrt{\log\log N}\) instead of \(\sqrt{\log N}\). \(H\) constant: both sides vanish.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters, for context (the statement does not depend on the pair), with \(N\) large: \(j=1\) and \(H\) the indicator of the left half, \(V_1(H)\le3\); the bound is now \(3L\sqrt N(1+\sqrt{1+\log\log N})\), where lem:mesh-function-discrepancy gave \(3L\sqrt N(1+\sqrt{\log N})\).

Architecture test

Architecture test. Not decided. The third route review's test (the class bound at \((4,4)\) for two gradient levels, constant independent of the levels) was attempted through the cascade; neither outcome occurred. What was tried: for a closed chain \(Z\) on \(\mathcal G_{j_m}\) and geometric levels \(j_1<\dots<j_m\) with \(j_{k+1}\ge2j_k\) (so \(m\) is at most of order \(\log\log N\)), split \(Z\) at level \(m\) into its part covered by whole edges of \(\mathcal G_{j_{m-1}}\) (each such edge \(E\) taken with the median of the coefficients of its sub-edges, which makes the uncovered mass \(\rho_m(Z)\) subadditive in \(Z\)) and the rest \(R\), whose boundary lies on vertices of \(\mathcal G_{j_{m-1}}\); \(R\) splits into paths between such vertices and cycles, and each path \(P\) is replaced by a staircase \(S_P\) on \(\mathcal G_{j_{m-1}}\), as in lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy. The fine chains \(P-S_P\) and the cycles pay the level-\(m\) factor on mass at most \(2\rho_m(Z)\), and the coarse chain \(Z'=(\text{covered part})+\sum S_P\) goes to level \(m-1\). Repeating gives each level \(k\) a charge on \(\rho_k\) of the current chain, but the staircases \(S_P\), laid on \(\mathcal G_{j_{m-1}}\) through arbitrary vertices of that grid, need not be covered at any coarser level, so their mass re-enters the uncovered part at every later step: the bookkeeping attempted gives \(\rho_k(Z')\le\rho_k(Z)+2\rho_m(Z)\), and iterated over the levels the coefficient of the finest mass grows geometrically, up to \(3^m\), a power of \(\log N\) when \(m\) is of order \(\log\log N\). That growth is an artifact of this bookkeeping, not a demonstrated growth: a staircase is never longer than its path, so the masses stay bounded by \(\|Z\|\); the real defect is locality, since a staircase's mass is charged at coarser levels far from the edges that produced it. It stopped there; routing staircases through coarser grids makes detours longer than short paths (the reviewer's check), so no repair of the curve cascade was found. The alternative named next is to telescope the function instead of the curves: \(H=H_1+\sum_k(H_k-H_{k-1})\), \(H_k\) the averages of \(H\) on the graded partition coarsened to cells of side at least \(2^{-j_k}\), each difference bounded by lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy between \(j_{k-1}\) and \(j_k\); it needs that coarsening by block averages does not increase the variation beyond a constant, locally, which was not attempted.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py "log log N deviation union bound Leighton Shor mesh concentration sqrt j plus log log N" returned lem:mesh-function-discrepancy, lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split and lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy, the statements this one refines. tools/claim_resemblance.py on the spec: lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound (0.27), lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split (0.26) and conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy (0.25), none a deviation bound.

New mathematics. lem:loglog-mesh-deviation: the deviation term of the mesh and two-mesh lemmas is \(\sqrt{j+\log\log N}\) instead of \(\sqrt{\log N}\).

Goal progress. Unchanged: no implication toward conj:ultimate-matching changed. Bearing. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\), the averaged part of a class function at mesh \(2^{-j}\) has discrepancy at most \(L\sqrt N(j^{3/4}+\sqrt{\log\log N})\) times its cell variation, so for Lipschitz \(G\) (with part (c) of lem:mesh-function-discrepancy for the ring) at gradient level \(2^k\) with \(j\) of order \(k\) the averaged part pays \(\sqrt N(k^{1/2}+\sqrt{\log\log N}\,k^{-1/4})\) times its cost (an order estimate, constant not computed). For one level the earlier bound already gave the order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\); the gain shows only when summed over \(K\) levels, \(K\sqrt{\log\log N}\) against \(K\sqrt{\log N}\). Not covered. The remainders inside the cells and the assembly over levels.

Review. The stand-in review of the last checkpoint raised five objections, each right. (1) A record search that missed two bearing records got no prevention: queued as the build record-search-breadth. (2) The commit message of the reviewer-audit commit of this afternoon describes an AGENTS.md witness rule in wording that the rebase dropped for an equivalent rule from another session: recorded here as its correction; that commit changed only the reviewer definition. Meta. The objections share one way of working: answering a found fault by naming a rule or a future build instead of making the change that would have stopped it, because the change looked larger than the turn. It shows also in deferred owed attempts. The change made is to keep the inputs a prevention needs as soon as they arise (the reviewer reports, now saved), so a build is never deferred for want of them; AGENTS.md's rule that a prevention is built in the turn unless what blocks it is named is the standing rule.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Function telescoping: \(H_k-H_{k-1}\) vanishes on cells of side at least \(2^{-j_{k-1}}\), so it is constant on a graded partition with sides in \([2^{-j_k},2^{-j_{k-1}}]\), the two-mesh lemma's form; a local contraction of block averages, from the scale-invariant trace inequality \(\int_{\partial Q}|H-\operatorname{avg}_QH|\le C|DH|(Q)\) in a strip-averaged discrete form, would bound every jump of the coarsened averages and the interface term by \(C|DH|\) on adjacent cells, with no 2:1 balance; a point in the finest region would then pay \(\sum_{k\le K}(j_k^{3/4}+\sqrt{j_k+\log\log N})\lesssim j_K^{3/4}+K\sqrt{\log\log N}\). First test: two levels at \((4,4)\) on the ramp-plus-bumps example. Record. tools/search-claims.py "graded partition quadtree adaptive mesh dyadic refinement discrepancy" returned lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split and lem:mesh-function-discrepancy only; no trace-inequality claim on record.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the full entries of lem:mesh-function-discrepancy and lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy, and (4.107). One fresh-context medium reviewer read the draft, spec, commit message and those entries and found the proof correct: both deviation cases, the Bernstein constant \(1/16\) (with \(u\) below the variance bound, the denominator \(2(\sigma^2+u/3)\le\frac83\cdot2^{2k+2}N\)), the triple count and the convergence of the sum; it confirmed that every later step of both lemmas uses only the curve bound, and the Degenerate cases, Nonvacuity and Hypotheses. It passed it with corrections, all made in its wording: the form of (a) stated separately; the weaker failure probability stated and the events intersected; "variance bound"; \(L'\) a fixed Chernoff constant; the geometric spacing \(j_{k+1}\ge2j_k\); the \(3^m\) growth called an artifact of the bookkeeping with the defect being locality; the Bearing saying that one level gains nothing over the earlier bound; the Corollary's qualifiers; an unsupported clause dropped from the Meta. Discrepancy on dyadic partitions with each edge charged at its own level

16 October 2026 · Working proof; two-level test not decided · Telescoping over levels. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Where we stand. Unchanged: The entry adds a tool lemma and leaves the test undecided; the goal's state in Where we stand is unchanged.

Question. Can a cell function on an arbitrary dyadic partition pay each edge only its own scale's Leighton–Shor factor? The log-log deviation entry's reviewer sketched telescoping the function over levels with a local contraction of block averages; this entry proves it and uses it on the third route review's two-level test. The missing implication is conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\).

General statement. lem:local-factor-discrepancy, for every partition of the square into dyadic squares down to the Leighton–Shor scale and every function constant on its cells; and, for Lipschitz functions, every such partition in which neighbouring cells differ in side by a factor at most \(2\).

Local-factor discrepancy

Setting. \(X_1,\dots,X_N\) i.i.d. uniform on \([0,1]^2\), \(\ell_1\) the Leighton–Shor scale. A dyadic partition \(\mathcal P\) is a partition of \([0,1]^2\) into dyadic squares of sides \(2^{-i}\), \(1\le i\le\ell_1\); its edges are the maximal segments of cell sides containing no cell vertex inside, so an inner edge has the length of the smaller of its two cells, \(\ell(e)=2^{-i(e)}\). For \(H\) constant on the cells, \(\Delta_eH\) is the jump across an inner edge \(e\), and for an open set \(U\), \(|DH|(U)=\sum\ell(e)|\Delta_eH|\) over the inner edges inside \(U\). Write \(F(i)=i^{3/4}+(2+\log_2i)\sqrt{\log\log N}\), at most \(i^{3/4}+C(\log\log N)^{3/2}\) for \(i\le\ell_1\). \(\mathcal P\) is balanced if neighbouring cells (sharing a side segment) differ in side by a factor at most \(2\).

Statement. There is a universal \(L\) such that with probability tending to one: (a) for every dyadic partition \(\mathcal P\) and every \(H\) constant on its cells, \[\Big|\sum_{i\le N}H(X_i)-N\int H\Big|\le L\sqrt N\sum_{e\text{ inner}}\ell(e)\,|\Delta_eH|\,F(i(e));\] (b) for every balanced dyadic partition and every Lipschitz \(G\), \[\Big|\sum_{i\le N}G(X_i)-N\int G\Big|\le L\sqrt N\sum_{Q\in\mathcal P}F(i(Q))\int_Q(|\partial_1G|+|\partial_2G|)+L\sqrt{N\log N}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal P}\operatorname{Lip}(G|_Q)\,\lambda(Q),\] \(i(Q)\) the level of the cell \(Q\).

Proof. We work on the intersection of the events of lem:loglog-mesh-deviation (for lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy at every pair of meshes, with factors at most \(f(j)=2j^{3/4}+\sqrt{\log\log N}\)), of lem:mesh-function-discrepancy, and of the per-cell event in the proof of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split, which covers every dyadic square of side at least \(2^{-\ell_1}\) (at most \(2N\) of them).

Three deterministic inequalities. They are one-dimensional variation arguments and hold for any \(H\) of bounded variation, with \(|DH|_1(U)=|D_1H|(U)+|D_2H|(U)\) (for a cell function the edge sum above, for a Lipschitz function \(\int_U(|\partial_1H|+|\partial_2H|)\)); the squares below are dyadic. (A) For a dyadic square \(B\) of side \(s\) and squares \(R_t\subset B\) of sides \(s_t\) along one side \(\sigma\) of \(B\), with disjoint projections onto \(\sigma\): \(\sum_ts_t|\operatorname{avg}_{R_t}H-\operatorname{avg}_BH|\le|DH|_1(B)\). Proof: for almost every \(x\in\sigma\), let \(h_x\) be \(H\) along the segment of \(B\) perpendicular to \(\sigma\) at \(x\), \(m(x)\) its average and \(\operatorname{Var}\) the variation; then \(|h_x(t)-\operatorname{avg}_BH|\le\operatorname{Var}(h_x)+|m(x)-\frac1s\int m|\le\operatorname{Var}(h_x)+\operatorname{Var}(m)\), and \(\operatorname{Var}(m)\le\frac1s\int_0^s\operatorname{Var}(\text{row at depth }t)\,dt\). Averaging over \(R_t\) and multiplying by \(s_t\) gives \(s_t|\operatorname{avg}_{R_t}H-\operatorname{avg}_BH|\le\int_{\pi(R_t)}(\operatorname{Var}(h_x)+\operatorname{Var}(m))\,dx\); summing over \(t\), \(\le\int_\sigma\operatorname{Var}(h_x)\,dx+s\operatorname{Var}(m)\le|D_\perp H|(B)+|D_\parallel H|(B)=|DH|(B)\). (B) For two adjacent squares \(R,R'\) of side \(s\) sharing a side: \(s|\operatorname{avg}_RH-\operatorname{avg}_{R'}H|\le|DH|(R\cup R'\cup\text{shared side})\), by the translation sweep. (C) For adjacent squares \(Q\) of side \(s\) and \(q\) of side \(s_q\le s\), with \(R\subset Q\) the square of side \(s_q\) beside \(q\): \(s_q|\operatorname{avg}_QH-\operatorname{avg}_qH|\le s_q|\operatorname{avg}_QH-\operatorname{avg}_RH|+|DH|(R\cup q\cup e)\), by (B); summed over the \(q\) along one side of \(Q\), the first terms are at most \(|DH|(Q)\) by (A).

Telescoping. Let \(j_k=\min(2^{k-1},\ell_1)\), \(k=1,\dots,m\), \(m=\lceil\log_2\ell_1\rceil+1\). Let \(\mathcal P_k\) consist of the cells of \(\mathcal P\) of side at least \(2^{-j_k}\) and the dyadic squares of side \(2^{-j_k}\) that contain smaller cells (the refined blocks of level \(k\)), and \(H_k\) the averages of \(H\) on \(\mathcal P_k\); \(H_m=H\), and \(H=H_1+\sum_{k\ge2}D_k\), \(D_k=H_k-H_{k-1}\). \(D_k\) vanishes on every cell of \(\mathcal P\) of side at least \(2^{-j_{k-1}}\); on a refined block \(B\) of level \(k-1\) it is \(H_k-\operatorname{avg}_BH\). So \(D_k\) is constant on a graded partition with sides in \([2^{-j_k},2^{-j_{k-1}}]\) (the cells of \(\mathcal P_k\) inside refined blocks, and the cells of \(\mathcal G_{j_{k-1}}\) elsewhere), on whose coarse edges it is \(0\) on both sides. lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy with \(c=0\) and the smaller deviation gives \(|\sum_iD_k(X_i)-N\int D_k|\le2L\sqrt Nf(j_k)V^0(D_k)\). The jumps of \(D_k\): inside a refined block \(B\), those of \(H_k\), bounded by (B) and (C) applied to averages of \(H\) over the cells of \(\mathcal P_k\) in \(B\); on \(\partial B\) and on \(\partial[0,1]^2\), the two values \(H_k(q)-\operatorname{avg}_BH\) on either side, each summed along the side by (A). Each cell has four sides, so \(V^0(D_k)\le C\sum_B|DH|(B)\), over the refined blocks \(B\) of level \(k-1\). An inner edge \(e\) of \(\mathcal P\) inside such a \(B\) lies between cells smaller than \(B\), so \(i(e)>j_{k-1}\); hence \(e\) is counted only for levels \(k\le\kappa(e)\), \(\kappa(e)\) the least \(k\) with \(j_k\ge i(e)\), once each. Since \(j_\kappa\le2i(e)\) and the \(j_k\) double up to the cap at \(\ell_1\), \(\sum_{k\le\kappa}2j_k^{3/4}\le2j_\kappa^{3/4}+2j_{\kappa-1}^{3/4}/(1-2^{-3/4})\le C\,i(e)^{3/4}\); and \(\kappa\le\log_2i(e)+2\), so \(\sum_{k\le\kappa}\sqrt{\log\log N}\le(2+\log_2i(e))\sqrt{\log\log N}\). Finally \(H_1\) is constant on the four quarters: by lem:mesh-function-discrepancy (b) with the smaller deviation and its part (c), \(|\sum_iH_1(X_i)-N\int H|\le L\sqrt Nf(1)\cdot4V^{\mathrm{in}}_1(H_1)\), and (B) bounds \(V^{\mathrm{in}}_1(H_1)\) by \(2|DH|([0,1]^2)\). Summing gives (a).

Part (b). Write \(G=E_{\mathcal P}G+R\). For the averages, (B) and (C) applied to \(G\) (whose \(|DG|_1\) is \(\int(|\partial_1G|+|\partial_2G|)\)) bound each jump \(\ell(e)|\Delta_eE_{\mathcal P}G|\) by a constant times the gradient mass of the two cells beside \(e\), each charged at most a bounded number of times; in a balanced partition \(i(e)\) is within \(1\) of the levels of both cells, so (a) gives the first term. The remainder is bounded cell by cell exactly as in lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split, whose per-cell event covers every cell of \(\mathcal P\): \(|\sum_{X_i\in Q}R(X_i)|\le L\operatorname{Lip}(G|_Q)\lambda(Q)\sqrt{N\log N}\). \(\square\)

Hypotheses, each with its step. \(X_i\) i.i.d. uniform and cells of side at least \(2^{-\ell_1}\): the events used, through lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy, lem:mesh-function-discrepancy and the per-cell bound. \(H\) constant on cells: the layer cakes inside those lemmas. Dyadic cells: the coarsenings \(\mathcal P_k\) are partitions because dyadic squares are nested or disjoint. Balance, in (b) only: to compare the factor of an edge with those of its two cells, and to bound the multiplicity with which each cell's gradient mass is charged when each jump is bounded separately. (A) does not need \(H\) constant on cells (it holds for functions of bounded variation), and in (b) the remainder needs \(G\) Lipschitz only on each cell; for such \(G\) the same proof holds with the jumps of \(G\) across edges added to \(|DG|_1\) at their edges' factors. The conclusions hold for all partitions and functions simultaneously. Two items the two-mesh partition entry left open are settled here: the per-cell remainder on mixed partitions (the per-cell event covers every dyadic square down to the scale), and the boundary ring on mixed partitions, which (a) does not need since it has no boundary term.

Baseline. The bound needing no idea is lem:mesh-function-discrepancy (b) at the finest mesh the partition uses, \(2^{-j_{\max}}\): every edge then pays \(j_{\max}^{3/4}\). This lemma adds that each edge pays only its own level's factor, so coarse parts of a partition are charged as if the fine parts were absent.

Degenerate cases. \(\mathcal P=\mathcal G_j\): every inner edge has \(i(e)=j\), and (a) is lem:mesh-function-discrepancy (b) with the smaller deviation and the ring absorbed, up to \((2+\log_2j)\sqrt{\log\log N}\) in place of \(\sqrt{\log\log N}\); at small \(j\) it is weaker than lem:loglog-mesh-deviation's (b) by that \(\log j\). \(H\) constant: both sides vanish. A partition with one tiny cell: only the edges of that cell pay its factor.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters, for context (the lemma does not depend on the pair), with \(N\) large: \(\mathcal P\) the four quarters with the lower left one replaced by its cells of side \(2^{-j}\), \(j=\lfloor\frac13\log_2N\rfloor\), and \(H\) the indicator of the upper right quarter plus that of the lower left cell of side \(2^{-j}\): its inner edges are the two inner sides of the upper right quarter, of factor \(F(1)\), and the two inner sides of the small cell, of factor \(F(j)\), so (a) gives at most \(L\sqrt N(F(1)+2\cdot2^{-j}F(j))\), where lem:mesh-function-discrepancy at mesh \(2^{-j}\) charges the quarter's sides \(j^{3/4}\). For (b): \(\mathcal P\) the four quarters (balanced) and \(G(x)=x_1\); the bound is \(L\sqrt NF(1)+L\sqrt{N\log N}\).

Architecture test

Architecture test. Not decided. The third route review's test (the class bound at \((4,4)\) for two gradient levels \(y_1<y_2\), constant independent of the levels) was attempted with part (b); neither outcome occurred, and the attempt located the remaining condition. Take a balanced partition whose cells have level about \(j^*(y_2)\) on the level-\(y_2\) support \(S_2\), about \(j^*(y_1)\) on the rest of the level-\(y_1\) support \(S_1\) (with \(j^*(y)=\min(\ell_1,\lfloor(\log N)^{2/3}(\log y)^{1/3}\rfloor)\) as in lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split), and graded rings between. By order estimates (constants not computed): at its own level each part pays \(F(j^*(y))\le C\sqrt{\log N}(\log y)^{1/4}\) per unit of gradient mass, within a constant of its cost weight \((\log y)^{1/4}\) times \(\sqrt{\log N}\), so the first term of (b) is of order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) times the cost, except for the level-\(y_1\) mass lying in ring cells of level above \(j^*(y_1)\), which pays \(F(i)\) there; the second term is of order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) times the cost when each level's support is thick at its own cell size, as in lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split. So the two-level bound follows, at constant independent of the levels, under two conditions: (i) thickness of each support at its cell size, and (ii) the ring term \(\sum_{i>j^*(y_1)}F(i)\int_{\text{cells of level }i}|\nabla G|_1\) on \(S_1\) of order \(\sqrt{\log N}(\log y_1)^{1/4}y_1\lambda(S_1)\). Condition (ii) can fail: for a level-\(y_1\) ramp with \(y_1\) bounded and \(n\) level-\(y_2\) bumps spread at spacing \(n^{-1/2}\) finer than \(2^{-j^*(y_1)}\), the rings cover the square up to level about \(\frac12\log_2n\), and the ramp's mass pays about \((\log n)^{3/4}\), above \(\sqrt{\log N}(\log y_1)^{1/4}\) by up to \((\log N)^{1/4}\) when \(n\) is a power of \(N\) (an order computation, not a lower bound on the discrepancy). No partition avoids this in (b) when the bumps' total area is small (the reviewer's check, unverified): a cell of side \(2^{-j^*(y_1)}\) containing bumps pays \(y_2\) times its area in the second term of (b), so cells are forced down to the bump spacing; this concerns the bound (b), not every method. In that example the discrepancy is linear in \(G\): \(G\) is the ramp plus the bumps, each piece satisfies (i) and (ii) alone, and the costs add up to a constant, not by alignment (the bump gradients change sign) but because off the bumps \(G\) is the ramp, on them \(|\nabla G|\ge|\nabla(\text{bump})|/2\) once \(y_2\gg y_1\), and \(\psi_4^*(2s)\le C\psi_4^*(s)\) for large \(s\). The thickness condition (i) is a different matter: a single band of level \(y\) and width \(2^{-j}\) with \(j^*(y)<j\le\ell_1\) along a long curve keeps a fixed fraction of its gradient on a thin set under every split into boundedly many pieces (the reviewer's observation, unverified), so the upper bound pays \(j^{3/4}/(\sqrt{\log N}(\log y)^{1/4})>1\) times the target there; whether the discrepancy reaches that is what lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound (vertically scaled tents) and lem:band-overlap-penalty (band functions) bear on. It stopped there: whether every class function splits into pieces satisfying (ii) with costs adding up to a constant was not decided, and whether thin bands beyond \(j^*(y)\) exceed the target, which would make thickness a property of the class rather than of the method, was not checked against those two lemmas.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py "trace inequality block average telescoping graded partition local factor edge level discrepancy" returned prop:retained-product-degree, conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy and cor:graph-input-common-target, none a local-factor bound; after the review, "thin band tent class lower bound vertical scaling Leighton Shor width gradient level" returned lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound, lem:band-overlap-penalty, thm:below-curve-matching-exclusion and lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy, the first two bearing on the thickness condition (cited in the Architecture test). tools/claim_resemblance.py on the spec: lem:two-mesh-partition-discrepancy (0.47), lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split (0.46) and lem:mesh-function-discrepancy (0.38), all related in the spec as the lemmas this one assembles and refines.

New mathematics. lem:local-factor-discrepancy: on any dyadic partition down to the Leighton–Shor scale, a cell function's discrepancy is at most \(L\sqrt N\) times the sum over inner edges of length times jump times \(F(i(e))=i(e)^{3/4}+(2+\log_2i(e))\sqrt{\log\log N}\), the edge's own level's factor; and, for a Lipschitz function on a balanced partition, the same with each cell's gradient mass at its own level's factor plus the cellwise Lipschitz term.

Goal progress. Unchanged: no implication toward conj:ultimate-matching changed. Bearing. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\), for every Lipschitz \(G\) and balanced dyadic partition, \(|\sum_iG(X_i)-N\int G|\le L\sqrt N\sum_QF(i(Q))\int_Q|\nabla G|_1+L\sqrt{N\log N}\sum_Q\operatorname{Lip}(G|_Q)\lambda(Q)\). Not covered. The choice of partition for a general class function and the comparison of both terms with its cost, and the conversion to the cell-smeared \(\mu_X-\mu_Y\).

Review. The stand-in review did not run at the last checkpoint; nothing is owed.

The same reviewer read the corrected draft and confirmed the sharper factor, the cap and the generalizations, asking for the remaining wording now in. Process audit. Dominant-level split and its bound on the Leighton–Shor witness

16 October 2026 · Working proof; against Lebesgue measure · Thin bands. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning effort set by the session).

Where we stand. Unchanged: The entry adds a refinement and a witness check; the goal's state in Where we stand is unchanged.

Question. The local-factor discrepancy entry's attempt needed each gradient level's support to be thick at its cell size, and its reviewer asked whether the class lower bound's tents are thin bands beyond the mesh \(j^*(y)\), which would make thickness a property of the class. The missing implication is conj:conjugate-class-discrepancy at \((4,4)\); this entry states the single-level bound in terms of the cellwise Lipschitz sum directly and evaluates it on the lower bound's witness.

General statement. lem:dominant-level-split, for every Lipschitz function of the class at \((4,4)\) whose larger partial is at least \(y\) on its gradient's support, and every mesh up to \(j^*(y)\).

Dominant-level split

Statement. There is a universal \(L\) such that with probability tending to one, for \(X_1,\dots,X_N\) i.i.d. uniform on \([0,1]^2\): for every \(y\ge e^{16}\), every \(j\) with \(1\le j\le j^*(y)=\min(\ell_1,\lfloor(\log N)^{2/3}(\log y)^{1/3}\rfloor)\), and every Lipschitz \(G\) with \(\int\sum_i\psi^*_4(|\partial_iG|)\le2\) and \(\max(|\partial_1G|,|\partial_2G|)\ge y\) almost everywhere where \(\nabla G\ne0\), \[\Big|\sum_{i\le N}G(X_i)-N\int G\Big|\le L\sqrt{N\log N}\,\big(1+\Lambda_j(G)\big),\] \(\Lambda_j(G)=\sum_Q\operatorname{Lip}(G|_Q)\lambda(Q)\) over the cells of mesh \(2^{-j}\). The same holds with \(\Lambda_j\) replaced by \(\sum_Q\operatorname{Lip}(G|_Q)\lambda(Q)\) over the cells of any balanced dyadic partition whose cells have level at most \(j^*(y)\), by lem:local-factor-discrepancy (b) in place of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split, the gradient-mass step being unchanged.

Proof. On the event of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split, the discrepancy is at most \(8L\sqrt N(j^{3/4}+\sqrt{\log N})\|\nabla G\|_1+L\sqrt{N\log N}\Lambda_j(G)\). Where \(\nabla G\ne0\), the larger partial \(t\) is at least \(y\ge e^{16}\), so \(\psi^*_4(t)\ge\frac12t(\log t)^{1/4}\ge\frac12t(\log y)^{1/4}\) (as in that lemma's corollary), and \(|\partial_1G|+|\partial_2G|\le2t\le4\psi_4^*(t)(\log y)^{-1/4}\le4(\log y)^{-1/4}\sum_i\psi_4^*(|\partial_iG|)\); so \(\|\nabla G\|_1\le8(\log y)^{-1/4}\). For \(j\le j^*(y)\), \(j^{3/4}(\log y)^{-1/4}\le\sqrt{\log N}\), and the first term is at most \(128L\sqrt{N\log N}\) (the step uses \(\log t\ge\log y\)); on a balanced partition the first term of lem:local-factor-discrepancy (b) is at most \(\sum_QF(i(Q))\int_Q|\nabla G|_1\le F(j^*(y))\|\nabla G\|_1\), bounded the same way. \(\square\) It refines the corollary of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split: that corollary asked each partial to lie in \(\{0\}\cup[y,2y]\) and bounded \(\Lambda_j\) through a thickness ratio; here only the larger partial is constrained, and \(\Lambda_j\) is kept.

Hypotheses, each with its step. \(X_i\) i.i.d. uniform and \(j\le\ell_1\): the event of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split, intersected for the partition version with that of lem:local-factor-discrepancy. The class's cost and the larger partial at least \(y\ge e^{16}\): the bound on \(\|\nabla G\|_1\). \(j\le j^*(y)\): the first term's comparison with \(\sqrt{\log N}\). \(G\) Lipschitz: \(\Lambda_j\) finite and that lemma's corollary. The conclusion holds for all such \(y\), \(j\) and \(G\) simultaneously.

Baseline. The bound needing no idea for the lemma is lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split itself with \(\|\nabla G\|_1\) left as it is; the lemma adds that the class's cost, through the larger partial, makes that term of the target's order for every mesh up to \(j^*(y)\), so only \(\Lambda_j(G)\) remains to compare. For the witness below there is an elementary baseline (the reviewer's): it is \((A/w)\int_0^w1_{S(f+u)}\,du\), so its discrepancy is at most \(A\sup_u|\sum_i1_{S(f+u)}(X_i)-N\lambda(S(f+u))|\); rounding each hypograph to the union of the cells of mesh \(2^{-\ell_1}\) below its graph costs at most the points and area of the cells meeting the graph, of order \(Nm\), and the union's boundary has length \(O(1)\) since \(\int|f'|\le1\), so lem:mesh-function-discrepancy at \(j=\ell_1\) gives \(O(A\sqrt N(\log N)^{3/4})=O(\sqrt{N\log N})\). The split's bound on the witness is therefore a check of the method, not new information about that function.

The lower bound's witness

Example. The witness of lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound at \((4,4)\) (its Step 2: \(\kappa=1\), \(w=\delta\) the reduction's grid side, \(A=(\log N)^{-1/4}/2\), \(G=A\min\{1,\max\{0,(f(x)+w-y)/w\}\}\) over the Leighton–Shor graph \(f\)) satisfies the hypotheses with \(y=A/w\), and \(\Lambda_{\ell_1}(G)\le297/L\le2.25\) for \(L\ge132\) and \(N\) large, so it has \(|\sum_iG(X_i)-N\int G|\le3.25L'\sqrt{N\log N}\), \(L'\) the lemma's constant: the split is of the conjectured order on this witness, against Lebesgue measure.

Proof. On the band \(\partial_2G=-A/w\) and \(\partial_1G=Af'/w\), so the larger partial is at least \(y=A/w\); \(s/33\le w=\delta\le s/32\) with \(s=L\sqrt{\log N/N}\), so \(y\ge\frac{16}L\sqrt N(\log N)^{-3/4}\) and \(\log y\ge\frac12\log N-\log\log N\) for large \(N\), which gives \(y\ge e^{16}\) and \((\log N)^{2/3}(\log y)^{1/3}\ge0.78\log N>\ell_1\), so \(j^*(y)=\ell_1\). Let \(m=2^{-\ell_1}\); since \(\ell_1\) is the largest integer with \(2^{-\ell_1}\ge(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\) (the setting of lem:mesh-function-discrepancy), \((\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\le m\le2(\log N)^{3/4}/\sqrt N\), and \(w\le\frac L{32}\sqrt{\log N/N}<m\) for large \(N\). On a column of cells of width \(m\) where \(f'\) is constant, equal to \(\sigma\), the band meets at most \(3+|\sigma|\) cells, each with \(\operatorname{Lip}(G|_Q)\le(A/w)(1+|\sigma|)\). The breakpoints of \(f\) lie on the grid \(2^{-cr-1}\mathbb Z\) with \(2^{cr}\le N^{1/100}\), so at most \(4N^{1/100}+2\) columns contain one (a breakpoint on a column boundary counted in both), each contributing at most \(3(A/w)m^2(1+\sqrt r)^2\) with \(r\le\log N\) (a breakpoint on a column boundary counted in both columns). So \[\Lambda_{\ell_1}(G)\le\frac Awm\Big(\int_0^1(1+|f'|)(3+|f'|)\,dx+12N^{1/100}m(1+\sqrt{\log N})^2\Big)\le\frac Awm\,(8+o(1))\le9\frac Awm\] for large \(N\), using \(\int|f'|\le(\int f'^2)^{1/2}\le1\). And \(\frac Awm\le\frac{(\log N)^{-1/4}}2\cdot\frac{33}{L}\sqrt{\frac N{\log N}}\cdot\frac{2(\log N)^{3/4}}{\sqrt N}=\frac{33}L\). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. \(G\) constant: \(\Lambda_j=0\) and both sides vanish. \(G\) at a single gradient level with a support thick at the mesh: \(\Lambda_j(G)\le2\sqrt2y\lambda(U_j)\), and the bound is the corollary of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split. All gradients below \(e^{16}\): excluded; such \(G\) is \(e^{16}\sqrt2\)-Lipschitz and (4.38) bounds it directly. Functions with some gradients below \(y\) and some above are excluded by the hypothesis on the larger partial.

Nonvacuity. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\): the lower bound's witness above, with \(L\ge132\) and \(N\) large, satisfies every hypothesis, with \(y=A/\delta\), \(j=\ell_1=j^*(y)\) and \(\Lambda_{\ell_1}\le2.25\).

Architecture test

Architecture test. Not decided. The third route review's test (two gradient levels at \((4,4)\), constant independent of the levels) was not decided; this entry examined the thickness condition the previous attempt left. It is replaced by the condition \(\Lambda_j(G)=O(1)\) at some mesh up to \(j^*\), which the class lower bound's witness meets although it is thinner than every allowed mesh. Where the condition fails, by an order computation (constants not computed): a band of width \(w\) near \(\delta\) at a small gradient level \(y\) has amplitude \(yw\), so the cost allows total length about \(1/(yw(\log y)^{1/4})\), many parallel or separate bands; at mesh \(2^{-j}\), \(\Lambda_j\) is about \((2^{-j}/w)(\log y)^{-1/4}\), and lowering it to \(O(1)\) needs \(j\) near \(\ell_1\), where the first term of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split exceeds the target by about \((\log N/\log y)^{1/4}\). That excess is of the upper bound, and the layer-cake bound (total variation of the profile times the Leighton–Shor set bound) has the same excess, so it is not specific to the split (the reviewer's check). Whether such families have discrepancy beyond \(\sqrt{N\log N}\) is a superposition question. Part (1) of lem:aligned-superposition-bound, the exact convex inequality, bears on it and reduces stacks of bands aligned in both partials to single bands (or does so at the penalty \(\sigma\) where their partials disagree in sign, cf. lem:band-overlap-penalty); its part (2), the \(1+o(1)\) gain, needs gradients at least \(N^\theta\) and does not apply at small levels; lem:band-overlap-penalty concerns bands of one vertical orientation and says nothing about alternating slopes. The reviewer's computation, unverified, settles one family: a sawtooth on one Leighton–Shor graph with teeth no wider than \(\delta\) has discrepancy \(O(\sqrt{N\log N})\); what remains is teeth of width between \(\delta\) and the mesh, and bands on many distinct graphs with alternating signs. It stopped there.

Record search. tools/search-claims.py "lower bound witness band upper bound tight cellwise Lipschitz constant area sum class order" returned lem:local-factor-discrepancy, lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split and lem:mesh-function-discrepancy, none a bound on the witness; a search in the words of the open case, "parallel bands alternating slope sawtooth superposition same gradient level", returned lem:band-overlap-penalty and two unrelated claims of other notebooks. tools/claim_resemblance.py on the spec: lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split (0.72, related in the spec as the lemma this one refines), lem:mesh-function-discrepancy (0.40) and lem:local-factor-discrepancy (0.39).

New mathematics. lem:dominant-level-split: for class functions at \((4,4)\) whose larger partial is at least \(y\) on their gradient's support, the discrepancy against Lebesgue measure is at most \(L\sqrt{N\log N}(1+\Lambda_j(G))\) for every mesh up to \(j^*(y)\); on the class lower bound's witness \(\Lambda_{\ell_1}\le297/L\), so the split is of the conjectured order there.

Goal progress. Unchanged: no implication toward conj:ultimate-matching changed. Bearing. On the curve's parameters at \((4,4)\), the class lower bound's single thin band is within the split's reach: it is bounded against Lebesgue measure at the order \(\sqrt{N\log N}\). Not covered. Functions with \(\Lambda_j\) large at every allowed mesh, several gradient levels, and the conversion to the cell-smeared \(\mu_X-\mu_Y\).

Review. The stand-in review did not run at the last checkpoint; nothing is owed.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • A one-graph band lemma: for \(0\le G\le a\) supported in a band of width \(W\) along one Leighton–Shor graph, \(|\sum_iG(X_i)-N\int G|\le a(2\,\mathrm{LS}+NW)\), \(\mathrm{LS}=L\sqrt N(\log N)^{3/4}\), from \(-N\int G\le\operatorname{disc}(G)\le a(D(W)-D(0))+NaW\); it settles every sawtooth on one graph with teeth of width at most about \(\delta\), leaving teeth between \(\delta\) and the mesh and bands on many graphs. The reviewer's search "Leighton Shor upper bound hypograph discrepancy curves" found no registered hypograph upper bound; the rounding of the Baseline paragraph with lem:mesh-function-discrepancy at \(j=\ell_1\) gives the bound \(D(W)-D(0)\) needs, checked by the reviewer.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the full entries of lem:cellwise-lipschitz-split and lem:leighton-shor-class-lower-bound (its Steps 1–2). One fresh-context medium reviewer read the draft, spec, commit message and those entries, found the lemma's proof and the witness arithmetic correct (\(\delta\in[s/33,s/32]\), \(y\), \(j^*(y)=\ell_1\) with \(0.794\log N>0.721\log N\), the column count, the integral, \(297/L\)) and no unused hypothesis, and passed it with corrections, all made: \(w<m\) from \(\ell_1\)'s definition; the breakpoint constants; one name for each constant in the spec and commit message; the two lemmas' bearing on the open case restated (part (1) of lem:aligned-superposition-bound only; lem:band-overlap-penalty about one orientation); the elementary baseline for the witness added; the Bearing narrowed to the single thin band; the free partition version stated; the refines edge limited to the corollary; the Degenerate cases split. The same reviewer read the corrected draft and confirmed the partition version (\(F(j^*)\|\nabla G\|_1\le C\sqrt{\log N}\)) and the baseline's rounding on the per-cell event, asking for three wording fixes, now in. Process audit. It proposed the title "Dominant-level split and its bound on the Leighton–Shor witness", which the title follows; strength count 3 on the first draft (the Bearing's general claim from one witness, a dependency's bearing beyond its hypotheses, the spec's "the open case") and 1 on the corrected one (part (1)'s reduction stated without its alignment hypothesis), all corrected; it found the elementary baseline uncomputed, the more general version unread although the resemblance tool listed it, and the obstacle blamed on the split without trying the layer-cake bound the brief named.
